## Section 8: Representation Question Closure

**What the legal framework provides or promises.** The federal Health Center Program under Section 330 PHSA establishes substantial structural infrastructure for safety-net delivery: community-board-governed FQHCs with 51% patient board members; sliding-fee scale for income-qualifying patients; Medicaid FQHC PPS reimbursement (42 C.F.R. Part 405 Subpart X); FTCA medical malpractice coverage; 340B Drug Pricing Program access; Ryan White HIV/AIDS specialty architecture; Title X family-planning architecture. The Community Health Center Fund provides approximately 70% of federal grant funding to FQHCs at $4.6 billion FY 2026 per 2026 Consolidated Appropriations Act. The Pennsylvania architecture adds state-level texture: PA Medicaid FQHC PPS, PA DOH licensing, and Philadelphia DPH safety-net clinic infrastructure.

**What PA-3 constituents actually receive.** Philadelphia FIGHT, Puentes de Salud, MANNA, RHD, FPCN, and additional FQHCs deliver comprehensive primary care, Ryan White HIV specialty care, bilingual community-tailored services, and 340B-discounted pharmacy access to PA-3 underserved populations. PDPH Health Centers 4/5/6/9/Strawberry Mansion deliver parallel safety-net primary care under city-operated architecture. Sliding-fee scale provides access to uninsured patients; FQHC PPS supports Medicaid-enrolled patient continuity; 340B savings support FQHC operations. Median FQHC operating margins below negative 2% with less than 90 days cash on hand per NACHC [Source 81]; cumulative federal-policy-cycle mechanisms operate concurrently against PA-3 safety-net infrastructure.

**What accounts for the gap.** Six structural features account for the gap between framework and received delivery: (a) cumulative federal-policy-cycle mechanism architecture operating concurrently in 2026-2027 (OBBBA Medicaid cuts + 6-month redetermination + 340B rebate pilot disposition + CHCF reauthorization uncertainty + provider-tax stepdown + Hahnemann-precedent fragility context) — the operational core of the MC55 PRIMARY HOM emergent-from-interaction shape diagnostic at SD5; (b) PA-3 sub-area-disaggregated data gap at FQHC and PDPH clinic levels; (c) FQHC capacity layer (median operating margins below negative 2%; less than 90 days cash on hand) creates acute fiscal vulnerability to any single mechanism plus cumulative vulnerability across mechanisms; (d) urban-FQHC exclusion from Rural Health Transformation Fund ($50B / $10B annually 2026-2030; rural-only [Source 72]); (e) noncitizen Medicaid restrictions effective October 1, 2026 (Section 71109) affect FQHC noncitizen-serving revenue [Source 74]; (f) PDPH city-operated clinic architecture differs from HRSA-deemed FQHC architecture, with different fiscal-vulnerability exposure but similar cumulative-mechanism exposure. The cumulative effect produces the MC55 PRIMARY HOM emergent-from-interaction shape — substantive safety-net delivery operates AND structural cumulative fiscal vulnerability under multiple federal-policy-cycle mechanisms operates simultaneously.

**What the gap means for the representation analysis.** Federal House representation engagement is directly consequential at each of the six mechanism trajectories simultaneously: (a) OBBBA technical corrections (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02; D9 SD4); (b) Section 71107 implementation oversight; (c) H.R. 7391 cosponsorship/voting plus HRSA 340B rulemaking oversight; (d) CHCF multi-year reauthorization at adequate funding levels; (e) provider-tax safe-harbor stepdown amendments; (f) Hospital Preservation / safety-net-institution-protection legislation. Single-mechanism federal engagement reduces individual mechanism magnitude but does not address cumulative-vulnerability outcome per the MC55 PRIMARY HOM diagnostic. PA-state-level texture engagement at PA Medicaid PPS, PA Act 77 interface, PA tort-reform context, and PA DOH / PDPH safety-net infrastructure is the complementary locus. The substructure lead-substantive observation that "financial-architecture covering doesn't reach core issues" applies here — addressing each mechanism's fiscal-architecture component does not address the cumulative structural-vulnerability outcome that emerges from interaction. The MC55 PRIMARY HOM shape preserves this disciplinary structure that resists premature analytical closure.

# D21 Healthcare Delivery — Sub-Domain 6
## Behavioral Health and SUD Delivery

**Date:** 2026-05-11
**Phase:** 1 (Per-SD Execution; fresh cycle)
**Domain:** 21 — Healthcare Delivery
**Sub-Domain:** 6 of 7
**Cycle position:** Phase 1 SD2-N batch — SD2-SD5 admin-reviewed-approved; SD6 of remaining SD6+SD7
**Methodology stack:** M0 v2.2 · M2 v1.2.1 · Skills v3
**Status:** Phase 1 draft — SD6 of SD2-N batch

---

## Section 1: Header and Regime Declaration

**Sub-domain scope.** SD6 documents the behavioral health and substance use disorder (SUD) delivery architecture serving PA-3 constituents: the federal Mental Health Parity and Addiction Equity Act (MHPAEA, 29 U.S.C. § 1185a) parity framework and the 2024 MHPAEA Final Rule + 2025 Trump-administration partial non-enforcement policy disposition; the federal Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration (SAMHSA) regulatory architecture under 42 C.F.R. Part 8 governing Opioid Treatment Programs (OTPs) with the February 2, 2024 Final Rule codifying COVID-era flexibilities permanently (effective April 2, 2024; compliance October 2, 2024; correction effective February 23, 2026 [Sources 88, 90]); the MAT Act (Section 1262 of Consolidated Appropriations Act 2023) eliminating the DATA-Waiver requirement for buprenorphine prescribing for opioid use disorder [Source 89]; the DEA-HHS jointly-finalized permanent telemedicine flexibilities for buprenorphine prescribing for OUD [Source 88]; the Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinic (CCBHC) Medicaid demonstration architecture and federal expansion grants administered by SAMHSA; the federal 988 Suicide and Crisis Lifeline architecture (under SAMHSA implementation per the National Suicide Hotline Designation Act of 2020); the federal 42 C.F.R. Part 2 SUD treatment record confidentiality framework (as harmonized with HIPAA under CAA 2021 amendments); the Pennsylvania Behavioral HealthChoices program — a **county-based BH-MCO carve-out** in which each PA county is assigned a single BH-MCO based on residence [Sources 86, 91]; the Philadelphia Department of Behavioral Health and Intellectual disAbility Services (DBHIDS) at 1101 Market Street, Philadelphia, PA 19107; **Community Behavioral Health (CBH)**, a 501(c)(3) nonprofit at 801 Market Street, 7th Floor, Philadelphia, PA 19107 — a **city-of-Philadelphia-contracted single BH-MCO** serving Philadelphia County exclusively under DBHIDS oversight since February 1997 (one of the first city-operated BH-MCOs in the US; **MC56 single-MCO concentration territory**) [Sources 86, 92, 93]; PA Act 98 of 2022 permanent removal of audio-only telehealth restrictions for outpatient psychiatric and SUD clinics [Source 86]; the PA Department of Drug and Alcohol Programs (DDAP) licensing architecture for SUD treatment facilities; the integrated primary-care–behavioral-health collaborative care model architecture; PA D-SNP behavioral integration (cross-reference D21 SD1 G21-SD1-04 MC54); psychiatric inpatient delivery (Friends Hospital; Belmont Behavioral Health; academic medical center psychiatric services at Penn, Temple, Jefferson, CHOP); psychiatric outpatient delivery; the Philadelphia opioid-overdose-crisis SUD treatment delivery infrastructure; and the harm-reduction architecture. **Boundary:** D3 Mental Health prior-method verified owns substantive mental health content base; D21 SD6 owns delivery-side architecture. D4 owns SUD substantive architecture; D21 SD6 owns SUD delivery side. D2 SD7 owns federal-disruption architecture for SUD federal-policy-cycle flow-through; D21 SD6 owns clinical delivery architecture.

**Regime declaration.** Internet-access regime. Current-state CBH operational status (CEO; financial scale; DBHIDS oversight; PH-MCO carve-out architecture per HealthChoices), MHPAEA enforcement administrative posture (2024 Final Rule + 2025 Trump-administration non-enforcement disposition; March 3, 2026 Tri-Agencies Fourth Report to Congress posture shift), SAMHSA 42 C.F.R. Part 8 implementation (April 2, 2024 effective; October 2, 2024 compliance; February 23, 2026 correction), MAT Act DATA-Waiver elimination, DEA-HHS permanent telemedicine flexibilities, 988 implementation, and PA Behavioral HealthChoices architecture claims are expected to be retrieved; specific figures carry sources per Standard 12. The MHPAEA statutory architecture, 42 C.F.R. Part 8 OTP framework, 988 federal architecture, and PA Behavioral HealthChoices framework are framework-level statutory references; specific section citations are sourced where claims are specific. Mixed-regime drafting prohibited per Standard 1.

**Analytical posture.** Multiple Both/And designations operative at SD6:

- **MC56 Both/And candidate (CBH single-MCO architecture).** Per D21 substructure §6 SD6 prediction, MC56 is the within-domain analytical-tension at SD6 — substantive city-of-Philadelphia-contracted single BH-MCO with documented DBHIDS innovative integration architecture (school-based behavioral health since 2003; Evidence-based Practice and Innovation Center 2013; CCBHC participation; D-SNP behavioral integration; older-adult CHC integration) [Sources 87, 92, 93] AND structural single-point-of-failure concentration risk plus single-MCO-architecture means no inter-plan competition pressure or member-choice architecture. The MC56 Both/And operationalizes throughout SD6 substantive content. Per admin note: MC56 may downgrade to non-PRIMARY if substantive content does not support PRIMARY designation; the substantive Both/And shape is documented in current source material and the PRIMARY-vs-non-PRIMARY designation is preserved at structural shape level pending substantive evaluation through Phase 1 draft.

- **MC32 / D6 MC32 federal/state EJ-divergence Both/And cross-reference (operative architectural context).** D6 MC32 federal/state EJ divergence affects SAMHSA targeted-population funding architecture per kickoff cross-reference direction. PA state EJ expansion (PA DEP Final EJ Policy January 3, 2026; PennEnviroScreen; OEJ at Special Deputy Secretary level; $267,825,172 RISE PA industrial decarbonization grants per D6 MC-04 April 28, 2026 [D6 MC-04]) AND federal EJ dismantlement (Trump EOs 14148/14151/14173 January 20-21, 2025; ~50 EPA EJ-focused staff terminated August 2025; EJScreen restricted; ~$20B IRA grants cancelled per D6 G6-SD5-01 [D] HIGH). The federal EJ administrative architecture thinning affects SAMHSA targeted-population funding, harm-reduction-grant architecture, and culturally-responsive SUD treatment funding architecture downstream.

- **MHPAEA enforcement Both/And.** Substantive federal parity framework (29 U.S.C. § 1185a; 2013 Final Rule operative; 2024 Final Rule effective November 22, 2024 with staggered applicability; CAA 2021 NQTL comparative analysis requirements operative) [Sources 94, 95, 97] AND Trump-administration partial non-enforcement policy May 9-15, 2025 covering portions of 2024 Final Rule applicable for plan years beginning on/after January 1, 2025 and January 1, 2026 [Sources 94, 95, 96, 98]; March 3, 2026 Tri-Agencies Fourth Report to Congress demonstrates shift in enforcement approach with DOL "not as active as previously" while state enforcement growing [Source 96]; ERIC v. DOL/HHS/Treasury case in abeyance per government motion pending reconsideration.

**Hold-open-magnitude territory** — none operative at SD6 (hold-open-magnitude project-wide instances concentrate at SD4 anchor community benefit commitment-vs-outcome HOM 7th-instance candidate and at SD5 emergent-from-interaction HOM 2nd-instance candidate; MC56 SD6 operates as Both/And without hold-open-magnitude designation).

**Thread declarations.** Four analytical threads operative at SD6:

- **D21-SD6-Thread A (federal-floor MHPAEA + SAMHSA + DEA + 988 architecture).** MHPAEA establishes federal parity floor for commercial and Medicare/Medicaid coverage of mental health and SUD services; 2024 Final Rule remains operative for 2013-baseline compliance with NQTL comparative analysis requirements while 2024 Final Rule portions placed under partial non-enforcement [Sources 94-99]. SAMHSA 42 C.F.R. Part 8 Final Rule (Feb 2024) codified COVID-era OTP flexibilities permanently: telehealth screening for buprenorphine initiation; take-home methadone doses (up to 28 days for stable patients; up to 14 days for less stable); audio-only and audio-visual telehealth screening for buprenorphine treatment [Sources 88, 100, 101]. MAT Act eliminated DATA-Waiver requirement effective Jan 2023; DEA Schedule III registration sufficient for buprenorphine OUD prescribing [Source 89]. DEA-HHS finalized telemedicine flexibility regulations making buprenorphine-related telemedicine prescribing permanent (was extended through end of 2025) [Source 88]. 988 Suicide and Crisis Lifeline implementation under SAMHSA per National Suicide Hotline Designation Act of 2020. 42 C.F.R. Part 2 SUD record confidentiality harmonized with HIPAA under CAA 2021 amendments.

- **D21-SD6-Thread B (Pennsylvania county-based BH-MCO carve-out architecture; CBH single-MCO Philadelphia concentration).** Pennsylvania operates the Behavioral HealthChoices program as a **county-based carve-out**: each county is assigned a single designated BH-MCO based on county of residence [Source 91]. Statewide BH-MCO landscape (5 organizations across 67 counties): Community Care Behavioral Health (CCBH; Allegheny, Berks, Centre, Chester, and many western/central counties); **Community Behavioral Health (CBH; Philadelphia County exclusively)**; PerformCare (Cumberland, Dauphin, Franklin, Lancaster, Lebanon, Perry); Magellan Behavioral Health of PA (Lehigh, Northampton, several northeastern); Value Behavioral Health of PA (rural central and northern) [Source 86]. CBH established February 1997; one of the first city-operated BH-MCOs in the US; contracted by City of Philadelphia DBHIDS [Sources 87, 92, 93]. CBH 2023 scale: 100,700+ Philadelphia Medicaid recipients using CBH services; total expenditures over $860,000,000; CBH provides behavioral health coverage for ~420,000 Medicaid recipients per DBHIDS [Source 87, 93]. 2026-27 PA budget proposes 15% increase to $4.4B in Medicaid capitation for behavioral and physical HealthChoices programs [Source 86]. The carve-out architecture creates auto-enrollment in CBH for all HealthChoices PH-MCO enrollees in Philadelphia regardless of PH-MCO selection (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-01 G21-SD2-06; D12 verified file line 111 structural distinctiveness).

- **D21-SD6-Thread C (D-SNP behavioral integration; CHC behavioral integration; D21 SD1 MC54 reciprocity).** PA D-SNP behavioral integration architecture (cross-reference D21 SD1 G21-SD1-04 MC54 PRIMARY Both/And on Medicare Advantage federal-administrative-vulnerability + substantive coordination) extends to BH integration through D-SNP plan design. Pennsylvania has aligned D-SNP requirements at the CHC-MCO selection process (D-SNP must be operational and MIPPA agreement in place by implementation date) [Source 95 cross-reference]. CBH operates a Community HealthChoices Team for senior 65+ members and members with permanent physical disabilities [Source 87], coordinating behavioral health care with CHC-MCO physical health and LTSS coverage (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-03 + D21 SD1 G21-SD1-04). The dual-tracked architecture (BH carve-out separate from PH-MCO and CHC-MCO) operates concurrently with the dual-eligible D-SNP architecture, creating multiple parallel managed-care interfaces.

- **D21-SD6-Thread D (commitment-vs-outcome HOM 7th-instance from SD4 + emergent-from-interaction HOM 2nd-instance from SD5 cross-reference architectural context).** SD6 BH-PH care coordination interface and CBH single-MCO architecture operate in the broader six-dimensional anchor accountability framework completed at D21 SD4 via INHERITANCE from D6 Synthesis Section 2. Anchor psychiatric services (Penn, Temple, Jefferson behavioral health departments; CHOP pediatric behavioral health) participate in the BH delivery architecture under MHPAEA parity requirements + 2024 Final Rule + 2025 non-enforcement disposition; CBH single-MCO contracts with anchor and community providers across the Philadelphia BH-MCO network. The MC53 SD4 PRIMARY Both/And (501(r) community benefit + medical debt) and the MC55 SD5 PRIMARY HOM emergent-from-interaction (FQHC + safety-net cumulative fiscal vulnerability) operate as architectural context but are NOT re-extended at SD6; cross-reference per Standard 9 specificity.

---

## Section 2: Authority Chain

The behavioral health and SUD delivery authority chain in PA-3 follows the federal-state-local layered architecture with substantial federal-regulatory density at MHPAEA / SAMHSA / DEA + substantial state-local density at PA Behavioral HealthChoices / DBHIDS / CBH single-MCO architecture.

**Constitutional Foundation.** U.S. Constitution Article I, Section 8 (Commerce Clause; Spending Clause; General Welfare Clause) authorizes federal MHPAEA framework, SAMHSA grants, Medicare/Medicaid behavioral health benefits, and federal SUD treatment regulatory architecture; Tenth Amendment reserves state SUD treatment licensing and BH-MCO contracting authority. ERISA preempts state regulation of self-funded employer plans for MHPAEA enforcement purposes; cross-reference D21 SD3 G21-SD3-04 ERISA-preempted MHPAEA enforcement architecture.

**Federal Statutory Layer.**

- **Mental Health Parity and Addiction Equity Act (MHPAEA; 29 U.S.C. § 1185a; 42 U.S.C. § 300gg-26).** Requires group health plans, individual market plans, Medicare Advantage, and Medicaid managed care to provide MH/SUD benefits at parity with medical/surgical — same financial requirements (deductibles, copays, coinsurance), treatment limitations, and non-quantitative treatment limitations (NQTLs including prior authorization, network access standards, medical-necessity criteria) [Sources 94-99]. Tri-Agency joint enforcement: DOL EBSA, HHS, Treasury. Consolidated Appropriations Act 2021 (CAA 2021) Section 203 added NQTL comparative analysis requirement. *Statutory stability HIGH at framework; administrative vulnerability HIGH at Trump-administration non-enforcement disposition for 2024 Final Rule portions* [Sources 94, 95, 96, 98].

- **MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule (89 Fed. Reg. 77586, September 23, 2024).** Tri-Agency final rule effective November 22, 2024 with staggered applicability: plan years on/after January 1, 2025 (initial compliance) and January 1, 2026 (additional standards including meaningful benefits, discriminatory factors prohibition, relevant data evaluation, comparative analyses) [Sources 95, 97, 99]. New regulations on content of comparative analyses, evaluation of outcomes data, prohibitions on discriminatory factors and evidentiary standards, requirements for meaningful benefits, and effects of non-compliance. ERIC (ERISA Industry Committee) filed lawsuit in U.S. District Court for the District of Columbia challenging Final Rule [Source 95]. **Trump administration partial non-enforcement policy announced May 9-15, 2025** covering portions applicable for 2025 and 2026 plan years [Sources 94, 95, 96, 98]; DOJ requested stay of ERIC litigation; case in abeyance pending Departments' reconsideration. 2013 Final Rule remains operative; CAA 2021 NQTL comparative analysis requirements remain operative; statute itself remains in place [Source 98]. **March 3, 2026 Tri-Agencies Fourth Report to Congress** demonstrates shift in enforcement tone with DOL "not as active as previously" while state enforcement growing [Source 96]. *Statutory stability MODERATE-HIGH; administrative vulnerability HIGH at 2024 Final Rule reconsideration trajectory.*

- **SAMHSA 42 C.F.R. Part 8 OTP Final Rule (89 Fed. Reg. 7528, February 2, 2024).** Modifies federal regulations governing OTP accreditation, certification, and standards for OUD treatment with MOUD (methadone, buprenorphine, naltrexone) [Sources 88, 100, 101]. Effective April 2, 2024; compliance October 2, 2024 [Source 100]. Codifies COVID-era flexibilities permanently: take-home doses (up to 28 days methadone for stable patients; up to 14 days for less stable); telehealth screening for buprenorphine initiation; audio-only and audio-visual telehealth platforms for buprenorphine treatment subject to HIPAA-compliant platform requirement; in-person components of full exam may be scheduled within first 14 days [Sources 100, 101]. Removes all language and rules pertaining to DATA Waiver per CAA 2023 [Source 100]. **Correction effective February 23, 2026** (91 Fed. Reg. 7456) correcting two outdated references [Source 90]. *Statutory stability HIGH; administrative vulnerability MODERATE-HIGH at SAMHSA implementation under Trump-administration SAMHSA*.

- **MAT Act / Section 1262 of Consolidated Appropriations Act 2023 (P.L. 117-328).** Removed federal requirement for practitioners to submit Notice of Intent (DATA Waiver) to prescribe buprenorphine for OUD treatment; eliminated patient caps; eliminated discipline restrictions; eliminated counseling certification requirements [Source 89]. Practitioners with current DEA Schedule III registration may prescribe buprenorphine for OUD subject to applicable state law. *Statutory stability HIGH*.

- **DEA-HHS Telemedicine Flexibility Final Rule (2025).** Finalized telemedicine flexibility regulations making buprenorphine-related telemedicine prescribing **permanent** (previously extended through end of 2025) [Source 88]. *Statutory stability HIGH at final rule; administrative vulnerability LOW-MODERATE*.

- **National Suicide Hotline Designation Act of 2020 (P.L. 116-172).** Authorized 988 Suicide and Crisis Lifeline as nationwide 3-digit dialing code; SAMHSA implementation administered through Vibrant Emotional Health contractor; state-level crisis-line architecture coordinates with 988 routing. *Statutory stability HIGH; administrative vulnerability MODERATE at SAMHSA implementation*.

- **42 C.F.R. Part 2 SUD Treatment Record Confidentiality.** Federal SUD treatment record confidentiality framework; harmonized with HIPAA under CAA 2021 amendments to permit single patient consent for full HIPAA-style use and disclosure; 42 C.F.R. Part 2 Final Rule (February 2024) implements harmonization. *Statutory stability HIGH; administrative vulnerability LOW-MODERATE*.

- **CCBHC Medicaid Demonstration (Section 223 of Protecting Access to Medicare Act 2014; expanded by CCBHC Expansion Act).** Authorizes Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinic Medicaid demonstration with PPS reimbursement architecture; SAMHSA administers CCBHC expansion grants. *Statutory stability HIGH; administrative vulnerability MODERATE at federal appropriation cycle for CCBHC expansion grants*.

- **One Big Beautiful Bill Act of 2025 (P.L. 119-21) behavioral-health-affecting provisions.** OBBBA Sections 71107 / 71109 / 71115 / 71117 / 71119 affect Medicaid managed care including BH carve-out architecture (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02; D9 SD4). MCO-tax tightening (Section 71117) effective July 4, 2025 with three-year CMS transition window affects BH-MCO architecture at PA-3 (CBH is a Medicaid BH-MCO subject to MCO-tax architecture). *Statutory stability HIGH at enactment; administrative vulnerability HIGH at CMS implementation*.

**Federal Agency Layer.**

- **Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration (SAMHSA), HHS.** 5600 Fishers Lane, Rockville, MD 20857. Administers federal MH and SUD grant architecture; Center for Substance Abuse Treatment administers OTP certification; Center for Mental Health Services administers MH grant programs and 988 implementation; CCBHC expansion grants. *per Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out: SAMHSA Administrator identification in official capacity deferred to source-grounded surfacing per Federal Register notices; named SAMHSA officials per cited Federal Register documents (e.g., Robert Baillieu, MD, DHSc, MPH, Center for Substance Abuse Treatment, per Source 90 February 23, 2026 correction)*. *Administrative vulnerability HIGH at Trump-administration SAMHSA policy posture*.

- **U.S. Department of Labor (DOL) Employee Benefits Security Administration (EBSA).** 200 Constitution Avenue NW, Washington, DC 20210. Joint MHPAEA enforcement with HHS and Treasury (Tri-Agency); ERISA enforcement for self-funded employer plans. *Administrative vulnerability HIGH at 2024 Final Rule non-enforcement disposition*.

- **U.S. Department of Health and Human Services (HHS), Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS).** 7500 Security Boulevard, Baltimore, MD 21244. Joint MHPAEA enforcement; Medicaid managed care oversight including BH carve-out architectures; Medicare Advantage MH/SUD coverage oversight. *Administrative vulnerability HIGH*.

- **U.S. Department of the Treasury, Internal Revenue Service.** Joint MHPAEA enforcement; CCBHC PPS tax architecture oversight. *Administrative vulnerability MODERATE*.

- **Drug Enforcement Administration (DEA), U.S. Department of Justice.** Administers Schedule III registration architecture for buprenorphine OUD prescribing post-MAT Act; jointly finalized telemedicine flexibility permanent regulations with HHS [Source 88]. *Administrative vulnerability MODERATE*.

- **Vibrant Emotional Health (988 contractor).** Administers 988 Suicide and Crisis Lifeline operational architecture under SAMHSA contract. *Administrative vulnerability LOW-MODERATE*.

**State Statutory Layer.**

- **Pennsylvania Mental Health Procedures Act (50 P.S. § 7101 et seq.).** Governs involuntary mental health treatment (302/303/304 commitment), voluntary inpatient psychiatric care, and Mental Health Review Officer architecture. *Statutory stability HIGH*.

- **Pennsylvania Mental Health Parity Act (PA Act 50 of 2004; 40 P.S. § 908-1 et seq.).** PA state MHPAEA implementation for commercial plans regulated under PA insurance code (cross-reference D21 SD3 G21-SD3-04 MHPAEA architecture). PA Insurance Department enforcement authority on PA-regulated commercial plans; ERISA-preempted self-funded plans federally enforced only. *Statutory stability HIGH; administrative vulnerability MODERATE at PA Insurance Department enforcement and at state-level parallel-state-enforcement growth pattern per Tri-Agencies Fourth Report* [Source 96].

- **PA Act 98 of 2022 (Telehealth permanence).** Permanently removed PA DHS regulations prohibiting payment for audio-only telehealth in outpatient psychiatric clinics and outpatient drug-and-alcohol clinic services. Practitioners no longer need waiver requests for audio-only telehealth when delivered in accordance with DHS telehealth bulletins. PA also defined telehealth within OTP rules to explicitly include HIPAA-compliant video and audio-only platforms [Source 86]. *Statutory stability HIGH*.

- **Pennsylvania Behavioral HealthChoices program (62 P.S. § 101 et seq. authorizing architecture; PA DHS administrative implementation).** Pennsylvania's Medicaid behavioral health carve-out architecture; each county assigned to a single designated BH-MCO based on county of residence [Source 91]. *Statutory stability HIGH; administrative vulnerability MODERATE at PA DHS BH-MCO contracting and at OBBBA-driven Medicaid disruption flow-through*.

- **PA Department of Drug and Alcohol Programs (DDAP) authorizing statutes.** Authorize SUD treatment facility licensing in Pennsylvania; SUD facility operation requirements; OTP coordination with SAMHSA federal architecture. *Statutory stability HIGH*.

- **Philadelphia Code Title 6 (Health).** Authorizes Philadelphia DBHIDS operational architecture; CBH contracting through DBHIDS. *Statutory stability HIGH*.

**State Agency Layer.**

- **Pennsylvania Department of Human Services (DHS), Office of Mental Health and Substance Abuse Services (OMHSAS).** 625 Forster Street, Harrisburg, PA 17120. Administers Pennsylvania Medicaid behavioral health architecture; oversees county BH-MCO contracts (CBH, CCBH, PerformCare, Magellan Behavioral Health, Value Behavioral Health). *per Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out: PA DHS Secretary and OMHSAS Deputy Secretary identification in official capacity deferred to source-grounded surfacing.* *Administrative vulnerability MODERATE-HIGH*.

- **Pennsylvania Department of Drug and Alcohol Programs (DDAP).** 02 Kline Plaza, Harrisburg, PA 17104. Licenses SUD treatment facilities; coordinates with federal SAMHSA; administers state-level SUD funding architecture. *per Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out: DDAP Secretary identification in official capacity deferred to source-grounded surfacing.* *Administrative vulnerability MODERATE*.

- **Pennsylvania Insurance Department.** 1326 Strawberry Square, Harrisburg, PA 17120. Enforces PA Mental Health Parity Act on PA-regulated commercial plans; cross-reference D21 SD3 G21-SD3-04. *per Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out: Insurance Commissioner Michael Humphreys per D21 SD2 Source 49 official capacity (Source 49 from SD2 carried forward).* *Administrative vulnerability MODERATE*.

**Local Statutory Layer.**

- **Philadelphia Code Title 6 (Health) authorizing DBHIDS architecture.** *Statutory stability HIGH*.

- **CBH single-MCO contract with City of Philadelphia DBHIDS.** Contractual architecture rather than statutory; renewable contract terms govern operational requirements. Cross-reference Philadelphia City Council oversight of DBHIDS budget appropriations.

**Local Agency Layer.**

- **Philadelphia Department of Behavioral Health and Intellectual disAbility Services (DBHIDS).** 1101 Market Street, Philadelphia, PA 19107. Contracts CBH as Philadelphia's single BH-MCO under PA Behavioral HealthChoices carve-out architecture; administers Philadelphia's behavioral-health-and-intellectual-disability service system; coordinates with PA OMHSAS, PA DDAP, and federal SAMHSA [Source 87]. *per Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out: DBHIDS Commissioner identification in official capacity deferred to source-grounded surfacing.* *Administrative vulnerability MODERATE*.

- **Community Behavioral Health (CBH).** 801 Market Street, 7th Floor, Philadelphia, PA 19107. 501(c)(3) nonprofit corporation; city-of-Philadelphia-contracted single BH-MCO since February 1997; one of the first city-operated BH-MCOs in the US [Sources 87, 92, 93]. CBH provides behavioral health Medicaid managed care for ~420,000 Medicaid recipients in Philadelphia per DBHIDS [Source 87]; 100,700+ used CBH services in 2023 with total expenditures of $860,000,000+ [Source 93]. 2018 budget over $900M; one of top eleven Philadelphia non-profits by income per Pew Charitable Trusts [Source 93]. *per Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out: Donna E.M. Bailey identified as CBH CEO per Source 93 (CBH Wikipedia citation referencing public records).* *Administrative vulnerability MODERATE — single-MCO architecture means no inter-plan-competition pressure*.

- **PA-3 SUD treatment infrastructure.** OTP methadone clinics operating in PA-3 (specific roster F-flagged, F21-SD6-01); office-based buprenorphine prescribing at FQHCs (cross-reference D21 SD5 — Philadelphia FIGHT, Puentes de Salud, FPCN, RHD; office-based prescribing at primary care providers in PA-3); SUD treatment facilities licensed by DDAP (specific roster F-flagged, F21-SD6-02).

- **PA-3 psychiatric inpatient infrastructure.** Friends Hospital (4641 Roosevelt Boulevard, Philadelphia, PA 19124); Belmont Behavioral Health (4200 Monument Road, Philadelphia, PA 19131); inpatient psychiatric departments at Penn (HUP; Penn Presbyterian; Pennsylvania Hospital), Temple, Jefferson, CHOP (pediatric); Horsham Clinic (suburban; cross-county boundary). Specific 2026 operational status F-flagged (F21-SD6-03).

**Constituent Layer.** Aggregate constituent profiles introduced in Section 5: PA-3 working-age adult navigating CBH BH carve-out + commercial-coverage MHPAEA-2024-Final-Rule-non-enforcement layer; PA-3 OUD patient navigating MAT/buprenorphine office-based delivery; PA-3 child navigating school-based behavioral health under CBH school-based architecture; PA-3 dual-eligible navigating BH carve-out + D-SNP behavioral integration coordination.

---

## Section 3: Pathway Tracing

The behavioral health and SUD delivery pathway for a PA-3 constituent depends on insurance type (commercial / Medicare / Medicaid HealthChoices / dual-eligible / uninsured), severity and acuity (routine MH need / SUD chronic / crisis / acute psychiatric), and treatment modality (outpatient psychiatric / outpatient therapy / SUD MAT / inpatient psychiatric / 988 crisis line / school-based BH / integrated primary care BH).

*Triggering condition.* A PA-3 constituent enters BH/SUD delivery architecture under documented pathways: (a) acute crisis presentation triggering 988 Suicide and Crisis Lifeline contact or emergency department presentation; (b) primary care BH screening identifying depression, anxiety, or SUD requiring referral; (c) court-ordered evaluation for involuntary commitment under PA Mental Health Procedures Act 302/303/304 architecture (50 P.S. § 7101 et seq.); (d) SUD treatment entry through OTP methadone, office-based buprenorphine, or specialty SUD program; (e) school-based BH screening (CBH school-based program since 2003) [Source 92]; (f) employer-sponsored EAP referral routing to commercial MH coverage under MHPAEA-required parity; (g) Medicare beneficiary BH need routing through Medicare Part B outpatient or Medicare Advantage; (h) Medicaid enrollee BH need routing automatically to county BH-MCO (Philadelphia = CBH); (i) dual-eligible navigating BH carve-out plus PH-MCO/CHC-MCO plus D-SNP coordination interfaces.

*CBH single-MCO carve-out pathway (Medicaid).* PA-3 Medicaid enrollee in HealthChoices is automatically enrolled in CBH for BH coverage regardless of PH-MCO selection by virtue of Philadelphia County residence [Source 39 SD2 cross-reference; Sources 86, 91, 92]. No member choice in BH-MCO at the county level — the carve-out architecture assigns CBH as the sole BH-MCO for all Philadelphia HealthChoices enrollees [Source 91]. Constituent contacts CBH at 1-888-545-2600 (Member Services) or accesses CBH provider directory; selects in-network BH provider; CBH administers prior authorization for outpatient MH and SUD services; CBH manages medication formulary for BH medications (cross-reference D21 SD2 — PH-MCO formulary handles physical-health medications; CBH formulary handles BH medications including SUD-MAT buprenorphine). The single-MCO architecture means CBH operates the entire Philadelphia Medicaid BH network: no inter-plan competition pressure; no member-choice architecture; CBH has documented innovation capacity through DBHIDS partnership but also single-point-of-failure risk profile.

*Commercial MHPAEA pathway.* PA-3 constituent with commercial coverage seeking BH services encounters the MHPAEA framework at: (a) plan-coverage scope (plan must cover MH/SUD if it covers medical/surgical); (b) financial-requirement and treatment-limitation parity (deductibles, copays, coinsurance, prior authorization standards at parity); (c) network adequacy parity (provider network access at parity); (d) NQTL comparative analysis requirements per CAA 2021 [Sources 94-99]. The 2013 Final Rule remains operative; the 2024 Final Rule remains effective November 22, 2024 with staggered applicability for 2025 and 2026 plan years but the Trump administration partial non-enforcement policy (announced May 9-15, 2025) covers portions applicable for plan years on/after January 1, 2025 and January 1, 2026 [Sources 94, 95, 96, 98]. State enforcement growing per March 3, 2026 Tri-Agencies Fourth Report to Congress [Source 96]; PA Insurance Department enforces PA Mental Health Parity Act on PA-regulated commercial plans; ERISA-preempted self-funded employer plans federally-enforced-only with non-enforcement disposition operative on 2024 Final Rule portions. Cross-reference D21 SD3 G21-SD3-04 for ERISA-preempted MHPAEA architecture.

*SUD MAT delivery pathway (office-based + OTP architectures).* For PA-3 constituents with OUD, MAT delivery operates through two principal pathways: (a) office-based buprenorphine prescribing — practitioner with DEA Schedule III registration may prescribe buprenorphine for OUD subject to applicable state law without DATA-Waiver per MAT Act / Section 1262 CAA 2023 [Source 89]; DEA-HHS permanent telemedicine flexibilities allow telehealth initiation and ongoing buprenorphine prescribing [Source 88]; (b) OTP methadone delivery — SAMHSA-certified OTPs administer methadone under 42 C.F.R. Part 8 architecture with take-home doses up to 28 days for stable patients / 14 days for less stable patients per 2024 Final Rule [Sources 88, 100, 101]; in-person physical exam components may be scheduled within first 14 days of treatment; telehealth screening permitted for buprenorphine OUD initiation per 42 C.F.R. Part 8 [Source 101]. PA Act 98 of 2022 permanently permits audio-only telehealth in PA outpatient drug-and-alcohol clinic services without waiver request [Source 86]. Naltrexone (third MOUD; oral or extended-release injectable) is non-controlled and may be prescribed by any licensed practitioner under standard prescribing authority.

*988 Suicide and Crisis Lifeline pathway.* PA-3 constituent in acute crisis dials 988; call routed to PA's regional crisis-line architecture under SAMHSA Vibrant Emotional Health contract; crisis counselor administers brief intervention; if higher-acuity intervention needed, routing to mobile crisis team, ED, or emergency stabilization. Philadelphia mobile crisis architecture coordinates with CBH for Medicaid-enrolled callers and DBHIDS for non-Medicaid callers.

*Inpatient psychiatric pathway.* For acute psychiatric crisis requiring inpatient stabilization, PA-3 constituent routes to: Friends Hospital (4641 Roosevelt Boulevard); Belmont Behavioral Health (4200 Monument Road); Penn Medicine inpatient psychiatric (HUP / Penn Presbyterian / Pennsylvania Hospital units); Temple psychiatric; Jefferson psychiatric; CHOP pediatric psychiatric. Voluntary admission under 50 P.S. § 7201; involuntary commitment under 50 P.S. § 7301/7302/7303/7304 (302 emergency 120-hour commitment; 303 extended commitment to 20 days; 304 to 90 days; 305 to 180 days). Mental Health Review Officer architecture administers commitment hearings. Cross-reference D21 SD7 for cross-cutting BH workforce and inpatient capacity context.

*Integrated primary care BH pathway (collaborative care model).* PA-3 constituent receiving primary care at FQHC (Philadelphia FIGHT, Puentes de Salud, FPCN, MANNA, RHD per D21 SD5) or PDPH clinic may access integrated BH services where the FQHC operates a collaborative care model: BH care manager embedded in primary care; psychiatric consultant supervising medication management; measurement-based care with depression/anxiety screening and stepped-care intensification. Cross-reference D21 SD5 FQHC BH integration; CMS Collaborative Care Model billing codes (CPT 99492-99494) operational for Medicare and most commercial coverage.

*PA D-SNP behavioral integration pathway.* PA-3 dual-eligible enrolled in D-SNP (cross-reference D21 SD1 G21-SD1-04 MC54) plus CBH BH carve-out plus CHC-MCO physical health coverage navigates three parallel managed-care interfaces. CBH operates a CHC Team for senior 65+ members and members with permanent physical disabilities, coordinating BH care with CHC-MCO PH and LTSS coverage [Source 87]. The aligned D-SNP architecture per PA DHS CHC re-procurement (D-SNP must be operational and MIPPA agreement in place; cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-03) extends to BH coordination at the D-SNP plan design layer.

*School-based BH pathway (CBH school-based program).* PA-3 child enrolled in Philadelphia School District with Medicaid BH need may access CBH school-based BH services launched 2003 [Source 92]; school-based BH clinicians embedded in school buildings deliver outpatient counseling and care coordination. Cross-reference D11 SD7 SDP-Penn partnership architecture; D11 SD3 SBHC architecture if applicable.

*42 C.F.R. Part 2 SUD record confidentiality pathway.* PA-3 SUD patient SUD treatment records protected under 42 C.F.R. Part 2; CAA 2021 amendments harmonized with HIPAA to permit single patient consent for full use and disclosure; SUD treatment information may flow to integrated care team under single consent. Cross-reference SAMHSA 42 C.F.R. Part 2 Final Rule February 2024 implementation.

*Decision points where the pathway breaks for PA-3 constituents.* The BH/SUD delivery pathway breaks down at: (a) CBH single-MCO concentration layer (no member-choice architecture; any operational disruption or coverage decision at CBH affects all 420,000+ Philadelphia Medicaid recipients with no alternative BH-MCO available); (b) MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule non-enforcement layer (commercial enrollees seeking 2024 Final Rule protections — meaningful benefits, discriminatory factors prohibition, relevant data evaluation — face Trump-administration non-enforcement disposition; 2013 Final Rule baseline remains operative but newer protections in abeyance) [Sources 94-98]; (c) network adequacy parity layer (documented commercial behavioral-health network participation rates systematically lower than medical/surgical despite MHPAEA parity requirement; cross-reference D21 SD3 G21-SD3-04); (d) OTP geographic distribution layer (methadone OTPs concentrated in certain Philadelphia sub-areas; treatment travel and capacity gaps F-flagged); (e) BH-PH coordination layer (CBH operates separately from PH-MCO and CHC-MCO; cross-plan care-coordination burden falls on beneficiary or care manager per D21 SD2 G21-SD2-06); (f) inpatient psychiatric bed capacity layer (PA-3 inpatient psychiatric capacity F-flagged for 2026 operational status, F21-SD6-03); (g) SAMHSA grant architecture flow-through layer (Trump-administration SAMHSA posture affects targeted-population funding for harm reduction, culturally-responsive treatment, and CCBHC expansion grants; cross-reference D6 MC32 federal/state EJ-divergence context); (h) 42 C.F.R. Part 2 / HIPAA harmonization implementation layer (SUD treatment information sharing operationally complex even after CAA 2021 harmonization).

---

## Section 4: Statistical Profile and Sub-Area Variation

**PA Behavioral HealthChoices statewide architecture.** Pennsylvania operates BH-MCO carve-out across 67 counties through 5 BH-MCO organizations [Source 86]: Community Care Behavioral Health (CCBH; western and central Pennsylvania including Allegheny, Berks, Centre, Chester); **Community Behavioral Health (CBH; Philadelphia County exclusively)**; PerformCare (Capital Region: Cumberland, Dauphin, Franklin, Lancaster, Lebanon, Perry); Magellan Behavioral Health of PA (Lehigh, Northampton, northeastern counties); Value Behavioral Health of PA (rural central and northern). 2026-27 PA budget proposes 15% increase to $4.4 billion in Medicaid capitation funding for behavioral and physical HealthChoices programs combined [Source 86].

**CBH operational scale (Philadelphia).** CBH established February 1997; one of the first city-operated BH-MCOs in the US [Source 93]. 2023 scale: 802,250 Philadelphians Medicaid-eligible per Wikipedia citing public records; 100,700+ used CBH services; total expenditures $860,000,000+ [Source 93]. 2018 budget over $900M plus assets over $160M; ranked in top eleven Philadelphia non-profits by income per Pew Charitable Trusts [Source 93]. Per DBHIDS Division of Community Behavioral Health official architecture description, CBH is responsible for behavioral health coverage for the City's 420,000 Medicaid recipients [Source 87]. The two enrollment figures (100,700+ active service users vs 420,000 Medicaid recipients covered) reflect the carve-out's auto-enrollment architecture: all Medicaid recipients in Philadelphia are CBH members regardless of whether they use BH services in a given year. CEO: Donna E.M. Bailey [Source 93].

**MHPAEA federal enforcement scale.** 2024 Final Rule effective November 22, 2024; staggered applicability for plan years on/after January 1, 2025 and January 1, 2026 [Sources 95, 97]. ERIC filed lawsuit challenging Final Rule [Source 95]. May 9-15, 2025 Trump-administration Tri-Agency partial non-enforcement policy covering portions applicable for 2025 and 2026 plan years [Sources 94-98]. DOJ requested stay of ERIC litigation; case in abeyance [Source 95]. March 3, 2026 Tri-Agencies Fourth Annual Report to Congress: DOL "not as active as they previously were" in MHPAEA enforcement; state enforcement growing [Source 96]. Advocacy organizations expressing disappointment: Legal Action Center, American Psychological Association, Inseparable, Mental Health America, National Alliance on Mental Illness, National Health Law Program [Source 95]. PA Insurance Department enforces PA Mental Health Parity Act (PA Act 50 of 2004) on PA-regulated commercial plans; cross-reference D21 SD3 G21-SD3-04. Specific PA-3 MHPAEA enforcement complaints and resolution disposition F-flagged (F21-SD6-04).

**SAMHSA OTP/MAT architecture scale.** 42 C.F.R. Part 8 Final Rule published February 2, 2024 [Source 100]; effective April 2, 2024; compliance October 2, 2024 [Source 100]; correction effective February 23, 2026 [Source 90]. DEA-HHS permanent telemedicine flexibility for buprenorphine prescribing [Source 88]. MAT Act / Section 1262 CAA 2023 DATA-Waiver elimination effective January 2023 [Source 89]. Specific PA-3 OTP roster, office-based buprenorphine prescriber count by sub-area, and methadone-treatment patient volume F-flagged (F21-SD6-01, F21-SD6-05).

**988 Suicide and Crisis Lifeline implementation.** Authorized under National Suicide Hotline Designation Act of 2020; nationwide 3-digit dialing operational July 16, 2022; SAMHSA administers through Vibrant Emotional Health contract. PA-3-specific 988 call volume and routing-to-mobile-crisis architecture F-flagged (F21-SD6-06).

**Philadelphia opioid-overdose-crisis context.** Philadelphia opioid overdose deaths and SUD treatment capacity context cross-reference D2 SD3 and D4 substantive territory. Specific PA-3 2025-2026 overdose-death magnitude F-flagged (F21-SD6-07).

**PA-3 sub-area variation.** PA-3 sub-area-disaggregated BH/SUD treatment utilization, OTP geographic distribution, office-based buprenorphine prescriber concentration, and inpatient psychiatric capacity are not retrievable from public-facing data products. Per Standard 6 the limitation is stated. Structural inference: Friends Hospital and Belmont Behavioral Health in North/Northwest; academic medical center inpatient psychiatric capacity in West Philadelphia Core (Penn; Penn Presbyterian; CHOP pediatric psychiatric) and Center City interface (Pennsylvania Hospital; Jefferson). OTP methadone clinic concentration patterns documented as following SUD-prevalence geography. PA-3 sub-area-specific magnitudes F-flagged (F21-SD6-08).

## Section 5: Aggregate Constituent Profiles

Profiles per M0 Standard 13 are aggregate, not named individuals.

**Profile 1: PA-3 commercial-insurance-enrolled working-age adult navigating MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule non-enforcement layer in South Philadelphia (MC56-adjacent + MHPAEA Both/And).**

*Constituent type:* Working-age PA-3 resident with self-funded ERISA employer-sponsored commercial coverage; income approximately $68,000; single household; depression diagnosis requiring outpatient psychiatry plus therapy; South/Southwest sub-area. *Triggering event:* New onset depression requiring sustained outpatient psychiatric care; insurance prior authorization requirements operative.

*Pathway through the institutional system:* Constituent accesses plan portal; finds limited in-network psychiatrist options (8-12 week waitlist common per documented patterns; cross-reference D21 SD3 G21-SD3-04); finds therapist with shorter waitlist; encounters NQTL prior authorization for medication management. MHPAEA 2013 Final Rule baseline operative requiring NQTL comparative analysis; 2024 Final Rule additional standards (meaningful benefits, discriminatory factors prohibition, relevant data evaluation) effective November 22, 2024 but **Trump administration partial non-enforcement policy May 9-15, 2025 covers portions applicable for 2025 and 2026 plan years** [Sources 94-98]. ERISA-preempted self-funded plan; PA Insurance Department lacks jurisdiction; federal enforcement (DOL EBSA + HHS + Treasury joint) operates under non-enforcement disposition for 2024 Final Rule additions. Constituent may pursue MHPAEA complaint at askebsa.dol.gov; private MHPAEA claim through ERISA Section 502(a).

*Illustrative figures with data provenance.* MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule effective November 22, 2024; staggered applicability plan years on/after Jan 1, 2025 + Jan 1, 2026 [Sources 95, 97; D]. Trump non-enforcement policy May 9-15, 2025 [Sources 94, 95, 96, 98; D]. March 3, 2026 Tri-Agencies Fourth Report demonstrates DOL "not as active as previously" [Source 96; D]. ERISA Section 502(a) judicial enforcement architecture [29 U.S.C. § 1132; D].

*Outcome.* Constituent receives covered MH services at MHPAEA-2013-baseline parity but at delayed access; 2024 Final Rule additional protections (meaningful benefits standard; relevant data evaluation) operate under non-enforcement disposition. The MHPAEA Both/And operationalizes: substantive federal parity framework continues operative AND structural enforcement administrative vulnerability operates at meaningful magnitude through 2026-2027 trajectory.

**Profile 2: PA-3 OUD patient navigating office-based buprenorphine MAT in West Philadelphia.**

*Constituent type:* Working-age PA-3 resident; OUD diagnosis; HealthChoices Medicaid (Group VIII expansion; income approximately $24,000); West Philadelphia Core sub-area. *Triggering event:* Decision to seek MAT after period of unmanaged OUD; primary care provider initiates buprenorphine prescribing post-MAT-Act-elimination of waiver requirement.

*Pathway through the institutional system:* Constituent presents at FQHC primary care (Philadelphia FIGHT or FPCN per D21 SD5 cross-reference); primary care provider with DEA Schedule III registration evaluates for buprenorphine OUD prescribing; **no DATA-Waiver required per MAT Act / Section 1262 CAA 2023** [Source 89]; provider may initiate buprenorphine via audio-only or audio-visual telehealth per SAMHSA 42 C.F.R. Part 8 Final Rule + DEA-HHS permanent telemedicine flexibility [Sources 88, 100, 101]; CBH BH-MCO architecture administers buprenorphine formulary authorization (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-06 BH-PH coordination architecture). PA Act 98 of 2022 permits audio-only telehealth in PA outpatient drug-and-alcohol clinic services [Source 86]. 42 C.F.R. Part 2 SUD record confidentiality protects SUD treatment information; CAA 2021 harmonization with HIPAA permits single consent.

*Illustrative figures with data provenance.* MAT Act / Section 1262 CAA 2023 DATA-Waiver elimination [Source 89; D]. SAMHSA 42 C.F.R. Part 8 Final Rule effective April 2, 2024 + compliance Oct 2, 2024 [Sources 88, 100; D]. DEA-HHS permanent telemedicine flexibilities for buprenorphine [Source 88; D]. PA Act 98 of 2022 audio-only telehealth [Source 86; D]. CBH single BH-MCO formulary authorization [Source 87; D].

*Outcome.* Constituent receives buprenorphine OUD treatment through office-based primary care provider with telehealth flexibility; SUD treatment continuity supported by PA Act 98 audio-only telehealth allowance plus DEA-HHS permanent telemedicine flexibility. The substantive federal SAMHSA architecture + MAT Act simplification operates well at this delivery layer; structural risk is at CBH formulary authorization and at OBBBA Section 71119 work-requirement architecture if working hours fall below threshold (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02 + Profile 4 in SD2).

**Profile 3: PA-3 child navigating school-based BH under CBH school-based architecture in North Philadelphia (MC56 substantive contribution).**

*Constituent type:* PA-3 child age 11 enrolled in Philadelphia School District (cross-reference D11 SD7 SDP architecture); HealthChoices Medicaid; ADHD and anxiety diagnosis; North/Northwest Core sub-area; family income approximately $34,000.

*Pathway through the institutional system:* School counselor identifies BH need; refers to CBH school-based BH program (launched 2003) [Source 92]; CBH-contracted school-based BH clinician delivers outpatient counseling and care coordination at the school building; medication management referral to community psychiatrist within CBH provider network; coordination with primary care pediatrician at FQHC or PDPH clinic.

*Illustrative figures with data provenance.* CBH school-based BH program launched 2003 [Source 92; D]. CBH single BH-MCO architecture serving Philadelphia [Source 87; D]. Cross-reference D11 SD7 SDP architecture for school-system-side delivery and D11 SD3 SBHC where applicable.

*Outcome.* Child receives BH services at the school building reducing access barriers; CBH school-based architecture is the substantive contribution dimension of MC56 — the city-operated single BH-MCO has built innovative integration architectures (school-based 2003; Evidence-based Practice and Innovation Center 2013; CCBHC integration; D-SNP BH integration) [Source 87] that competitive multi-MCO architectures may not have replicated. The MC56 substantive-contribution dimension operates strongly here; the structural single-MCO-concentration risk dimension is less acute for stable program operations.

**Profile 4: PA-3 dual-eligible navigating CBH BH carve-out + D-SNP behavioral integration + CHC-MCO PH coordination in Northwest (MC56 structural concentration risk).**

*Constituent type:* PA-3 senior (age 72); dual-eligible Medicare + Medicaid CHC; chronic depression plus history of alcohol use disorder; income approximately $19,000 (SSI plus small pension); Northwest sub-area. *Triggering event:* Sustained BH coordination needed across Medicare D-SNP, CHC-MCO physical health, and CBH BH coverage.

*Pathway through the institutional system:* Constituent enrolled in CHC-MCO (Keystone First / AmeriHealth Caritas Southeast Zone; cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-03) and aligned D-SNP (cross-reference D21 SD1 G21-SD1-04 MC54). For BH coverage, constituent is automatically a CBH member by virtue of Philadelphia residence [Source 91]. CBH operates CHC Team coordinating BH for senior 65+ members and members with permanent physical disabilities [Source 87]. Cross-plan care coordination falls on the CBH service coordinator, CHC-MCO service coordinator, and D-SNP care coordinator across three parallel managed-care interfaces.

*Illustrative figures with data provenance.* CBH single BH-MCO architecture in Philadelphia [Source 91; D]. CBH CHC Team for senior 65+ and permanent disabilities [Source 87; D]. PA D-SNP alignment + MIPPA agreement requirements (D21 SD2 G21-SD2-03; D21 SD1 G21-SD1-04). Dual-eligible coordination architecture spans BH + PH + LTSS.

*Outcome.* Constituent receives coordinated BH + PH + LTSS coverage at the institutional level; coordination quality depends on the three parallel service coordinator architectures operating in alignment. The MC56 structural-concentration-risk dimension operationalizes here: any operational disruption at CBH (financial; contracting; leadership; system) flows through to all 420,000+ Philadelphia Medicaid BH-enrolled members including this constituent with no alternative BH-MCO available [Sources 87, 91]. The MC56 Both/And operates: substantive integrated coordination at CBH CHC Team architecture serves this constituent AND structural single-MCO-concentration risk creates institutional-fragility profile that competitive multi-MCO architectures distribute across plans.

## Section 6: Conversational Narrative Note

The most analytically important feature visible at SD6 is the architectural distinctiveness of Philadelphia's behavioral health delivery. Pennsylvania's Behavioral HealthChoices program is a county-based carve-out — each county is assigned a single designated BH-MCO based on county of residence, separate from the physical-health managed-care architecture documented at SD2 [Source 91]. Philadelphia County's assigned BH-MCO is Community Behavioral Health, a 501(c)(3) nonprofit that the City of Philadelphia DBHIDS established in February 1997 as one of the first city-operated BH-MCOs in the United States [Sources 87, 92, 93]. This is structurally different from the four-PH-MCO competitive architecture at SD2: CBH operates as the sole BH-MCO for all approximately 420,000 Philadelphia Medicaid recipients [Source 87]; there is no member choice in BH-MCO; there is no inter-plan competition pressure; CBH has built innovative integration architectures over more than 25 years (school-based BH since 2003; Evidence-based Practice and Innovation Center 2013; CCBHC integration; D-SNP behavioral integration; CHC senior coordination team) [Source 87] that competitive multi-MCO architectures may not have generated, AND CBH operates as a single point of accountability whose operational disruption — financial, contractual, leadership, or system — would flow through to the entire Philadelphia Medicaid BH-enrolled population with no alternative BH-MCO available. The MC56 Both/And designation captures both halves.

The most common misunderstanding about mental health parity in 2026 is that the 2024 MHPAEA Final Rule has substantially improved enforcement against commercial behavioral-health network-adequacy gaps. It has not, in 2026, in operational practice. The Tri-Agency MHPAEA Final Rule published September 9, 2024 was effective November 22, 2024 with staggered applicability for plan years beginning on or after January 1, 2025 and January 1, 2026 [Sources 95, 97]. The Rule included new regulations for content of comparative analyses, evaluation of outcomes data, prohibitions on discriminatory factors and evidentiary standards, requirements for meaningful benefits, and effects of non-compliance — substantive expansions of the 2013 baseline parity framework. ERIC (ERISA Industry Committee) filed a lawsuit challenging the Final Rule [Source 95]. On May 9-15, 2025, the Trump administration Tri-Agencies announced a partial non-enforcement policy covering portions of the Final Rule applicable for plan years on or after January 1, 2025 and January 1, 2026 [Sources 94, 95, 96, 98]; DOJ requested a stay of the ERIC litigation; the case is in abeyance pending the Departments' reconsideration [Source 95]. The 2013 Final Rule remains operative; the CAA 2021 NQTL comparative analysis requirements remain operative; the statute itself remains in place [Source 98]. The March 3, 2026 Tri-Agencies Fourth Annual Report to Congress demonstrates a shift in enforcement approach with DOL "not as active as they previously were" while state enforcement growing [Source 96]. The MHPAEA Both/And operationalizes here: substantive federal parity framework continues operative at 2013 + CAA 2021 + statutory baseline AND structural enforcement administrative vulnerability operates at meaningful magnitude through 2026-2027 trajectory at the 2024 Final Rule expansions.

The human consequence visible in 2026 operates at three convergence points. First, the federal SAMHSA OTP / MAT Act / DEA-HHS telemedicine architecture has substantively simplified office-based buprenorphine prescribing — practitioners with DEA Schedule III registration may prescribe buprenorphine for OUD without DATA-Waiver and using permanent telemedicine flexibility, materially reducing the workforce-access bottleneck that constrained OUD treatment capacity in PA-3 for two decades [Sources 88, 89, 100, 101]. The substantive contribution of this federal architectural simplification is genuine and at meaningful magnitude. Second, the CBH single-MCO architecture has delivered innovation in school-based BH, integrated care, and senior CHC coordination [Source 87] that operates at meaningful population scale (100,700+ active service users in 2023 with $860M+ expenditures [Source 93]) — substantive contribution to PA-3 BH access at the Medicaid layer. Third, the MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule non-enforcement disposition and the broader Trump-administration SAMHSA + CMS posture on BH/SUD architecture operate as structural enforcement-and-resourcing vulnerability at the federal-floor layer; combined with the OBBBA Medicaid disruption (D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02) flowing through to CBH BH-MCO capitation architecture, the structural-vulnerability dimension operates at meaningful magnitude.

The most analytically important federal-engagement feature visible at SD6 is the multiplicity of trajectories operating concurrently. MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule reconsideration (Tri-Agency reexamination of enforcement program; possible modification or rescission of Final Rule portions) is the principal MHPAEA-side trajectory through 2026-2027. SAMHSA architecture under Trump-administration HHS posture affects CCBHC expansion grants, targeted-population funding, harm-reduction grant architecture, and culturally-responsive treatment funding — cross-reference D6 MC32 federal/state EJ-divergence context for the EJ administrative-architecture-thinning flow-through to SAMHSA targeted-population funding. 988 funding architecture under SAMHSA annual appropriation cycles. OBBBA Medicaid delivery-side flow-through to CBH BH-MCO capitation architecture per Sections 71107/71109/71115/71117/71119 (cross-reference D21 SD2). Federal House representation operates at each trajectory directly through appropriation, oversight, and statutory-amendment authority; PA-state-level texture engagement at PA Mental Health Parity Act enforcement, PA Behavioral HealthChoices contracting, and PA Act 98 telehealth implementation is the complementary locus.

## Section 7: Gap Analysis

**G21-SD6-01: CBH city-of-Philadelphia-contracted single BH-MCO architecture — substantive innovation + structural single-MCO concentration risk (MC56 BOTH/AND).**
*Finding — substantive contribution:* Pennsylvania's Behavioral HealthChoices county-based carve-out architecture assigns Philadelphia County exclusively to CBH, a 501(c)(3) nonprofit contracted by the City of Philadelphia DBHIDS since February 1997 as one of the first city-operated BH-MCOs in the United States [Sources 87, 92, 93]. CBH operational scale: 100,700+ active service users (2023); $860,000,000+ total expenditures (2023); ~420,000 Medicaid recipients covered by BH carve-out architecture (current per DBHIDS) [Sources 87, 93]. CBH innovation architecture documented: school-based BH program (launched 2003); Evidence-based Practice and Innovation Center (launched 2013); CCBHC integration; D-SNP behavioral integration; CHC Team for senior 65+ and permanent-disability members [Source 87]. CBH operates under DBHIDS oversight at the city-government layer with PA OMHSAS state-level BH carve-out contracting and federal CMS Medicaid managed-care regulation [Sources 86, 87, 91].
*Finding — structural single-MCO concentration risk:* Single-MCO architecture means: (a) no member choice in BH-MCO at the county level — all Philadelphia HealthChoices enrollees auto-enrolled in CBH regardless of PH-MCO selection [Source 91]; (b) no inter-plan competition pressure on network adequacy, formulary, or prior authorization standards within Philadelphia BH market; (c) any operational disruption at CBH (financial; contracting; leadership; system; capitation rate inadequacy) flows through to all 420,000+ Philadelphia Medicaid BH-covered members with no alternative BH-MCO available; (d) OBBBA Section 71117 MCO-tax tightening effective July 4, 2025 (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02) plus OBBBA Section 71115 provider-tax safe-harbor stepdown flow-through to CBH capitation architecture creates fiscal-pressure profile at the Philadelphia BH delivery infrastructure layer.
*Evidence type:* [D] for CBH single-MCO architecture and operational scale; [D] for PA carve-out architecture; [SI] for single-MCO-concentration-risk operational profile (structural inference from market-architecture principles).
*Confidence:* HIGH for structural feature; MEDIUM for forward fiscal-pressure magnitude pending OBBBA implementation through 2026-2027.
*Representation implication:* PA-state-level engagement at PA OMHSAS Behavioral HealthChoices contracting and rate-setting is the principal texture-level locus; federal House representation engagement at OBBBA technical corrections (Sections 71115/71117 affecting BH-MCO capitation architecture per D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02) and at CMS Medicaid managed-care oversight is direct. *T21-SD6-01: CBH contract renewal and rate-setting trajectory under PA OMHSAS + DBHIDS through 2026-2027.*

**G21-SD6-02: MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule Trump-administration partial non-enforcement policy operational impact on PA-3 commercial-coverage BH access (MHPAEA Both/And).**
*Finding:* MHPAEA statute (29 U.S.C. § 1185a) requires parity between MH/SUD benefits and medical/surgical benefits in commercial group health plans, ACA marketplace plans, Medicare Advantage, and Medicaid managed care. The 2024 Tri-Agency Final Rule effective November 22, 2024 with staggered applicability for plan years on/after January 1, 2025 and January 1, 2026 [Sources 95, 97] included new NQTL comparative analysis content requirements, meaningful benefits standard, prohibitions on discriminatory factors and evidentiary standards, and relevant data evaluation requirements. ERIC filed lawsuit challenging Final Rule [Source 95]. **Trump administration partial non-enforcement policy May 9-15, 2025** covering portions applicable for 2025 and 2026 plan years; DOJ requested stay of ERIC litigation; case in abeyance pending reconsideration [Sources 94-98]. 2013 Final Rule + CAA 2021 NQTL requirements + statute itself remain operative; March 3, 2026 Tri-Agencies Fourth Report to Congress documents DOL "not as active as previously" with state enforcement growing [Source 96]. Cross-reference D21 SD3 G21-SD3-04 ERISA-preempted MHPAEA architecture; PA Insurance Department enforces PA Mental Health Parity Act on PA-regulated commercial plans.
*Evidence type:* [D] for MHPAEA statutory architecture + 2024 Final Rule + 2025 non-enforcement policy + March 2026 Tri-Agencies report.
*Confidence:* HIGH for federal architecture and non-enforcement disposition; MEDIUM for forward enforcement trajectory pending reconsideration outcome.
*Representation implication:* Federal House representation has direct legislative authority on MHPAEA statutory amendments; oversight authority on Tri-Agency enforcement architecture and 2024 Final Rule reconsideration trajectory. PA-state-level engagement at PA Insurance Department PA Mental Health Parity Act enforcement is the principal state-level texture locus; state enforcement growing per March 2026 Tri-Agencies report. *T21-SD6-02: MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule reconsideration trajectory through 2026-2027; possible modification, rescission, or partial reissuance.*

*Verification update (2026-05-15): Phase 3 confirms Phase 1 framing on MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule Tri-Agency reconsideration disposition. ERIC v. DOL/HHS/Treasury case in U.S. District Court for the District of Columbia (Judge Timothy J. Kelly) placed in abeyance May 12, 2025; non-enforcement policy announced May 15, 2025 applies to portions of 2024 Final Rule "new in relation to 2013 Final Rule" until final decision in ERIC litigation + 18 months thereafter; 2013 Final Rule and CAA 2021 statutory NQTL obligations remain operative. Departments committed in joint statement to "reexamine current MHPAEA enforcement program more broadly" citing EO 14219 ("Ensuring Lawful Governance and Implementing the President's 'Department of Government Efficiency' Deregulatory Initiative") as policy basis. NPRM rescinding or modifying not yet issued as of verification date. Joint status reports filed every 90 days from August 7, 2025; latest report ~May 4, 2026 (specific contents not retrieved at this verification — institutional retrieval). Structural-direction of finding confirmed HIGH for federal architecture and non-enforcement disposition; MEDIUM for forward enforcement trajectory preserved. MHPAEA Both/And shape at G21-SD6-02 confirmed; parallel SD3 G21-SD3-04 commercial implementation Both/And confirmed. T21-SD6-02 carries to embedded Temporal-Changes Sidecar as pre-window-date-locked vector point per Standard 18.A with next observation point at next joint status report cycle (August 2026) and/or NPRM publication.*

**<a id="g21-sd6-03"></a>G21-SD6-03: SAMHSA 42 C.F.R. Part 8 OTP architecture + MAT Act DATA-Waiver elimination + DEA-HHS permanent telemedicine flexibility substantive simplification (operative substantive contribution).**
*Finding:* SAMHSA 42 C.F.R. Part 8 Final Rule (February 2, 2024; effective April 2, 2024; compliance October 2, 2024; correction February 23, 2026) codifies COVID-era OTP flexibilities permanently — take-home doses up to 28 days methadone for stable patients / 14 days for less stable; telehealth screening for buprenorphine initiation; audio-only and audio-visual platforms subject to HIPAA-compliance; in-person components within first 14 days [Sources 88, 90, 100, 101]. MAT Act / Section 1262 CAA 2023 eliminated DATA-Waiver requirement for buprenorphine OUD prescribing effective January 2023; DEA Schedule III registration sufficient; no patient caps; no discipline restrictions; no counseling certification [Source 89]. DEA-HHS permanent telemedicine flexibility for buprenorphine OUD prescribing finalized 2025 [Source 88]. PA Act 98 of 2022 permits audio-only telehealth in PA outpatient drug-and-alcohol clinic services [Source 86]. The substantive federal architectural simplification operates at meaningful magnitude reducing workforce-access bottleneck for office-based buprenorphine prescribing.
*Evidence type:* [D] for federal statutory and regulatory architecture across SAMHSA, MAT Act, DEA-HHS, PA Act 98.
*Confidence:* HIGH for federal architectural simplification; MEDIUM for PA-3-specific operational magnitude (F-flagged).
*Representation implication:* Federal House representation engagement at SAMHSA appropriation, CCBHC expansion grants, and 988 funding; SAMHSA administrative posture under Trump HHS is the principal forward-trajectory variable. The substantive contribution direction at this finding is positive in 2026; structural risk is at the SAMHSA-administrative-vulnerability layer for forward trajectory.

**<a id="g21-sd6-04"></a>G21-SD6-04: 988 Suicide and Crisis Lifeline PA-3 implementation status.**
*Finding:* National Suicide Hotline Designation Act of 2020 authorized 988 as nationwide 3-digit dialing code; SAMHSA implements through Vibrant Emotional Health contract; nationwide operational July 16, 2022. PA-3-specific 988 call volume, routing-to-mobile-crisis architecture, and crisis-line workforce capacity F-flagged (F21-SD6-06). PA mobile crisis architecture coordinates with CBH for Medicaid-enrolled callers and DBHIDS for non-Medicaid callers.
*Evidence type:* [D] for federal architecture; [F] for PA-3 magnitudes.
*Confidence:* HIGH for federal architecture; MEDIUM for PA-3 operational disposition.
*Representation implication:* Federal House representation engagement at SAMHSA 988 appropriation; federal-state crisis-line architecture coordination. State-level engagement at PA crisis-line workforce funding and mobile crisis architecture.

**<a id="g21-sd6-05"></a>G21-SD6-05: BH-PH care coordination gap at CBH carve-out + PH-MCO + CHC-MCO + D-SNP interfaces (substantive cross-reference to D21 SD2 G21-SD2-06).**
*Finding:* The PA carve-out architecture creates parallel managed-care interfaces for PA-3 dual-eligibles and complex-care patients: CBH (BH carve-out) operates separately from PH-MCO (HealthChoices Physical Health) and CHC-MCO (Community HealthChoices LTSS), with D-SNP behavioral integration adding a fourth interface layer (cross-reference D21 SD1 G21-SD1-04 MC54). The cross-plan care-coordination burden falls on the beneficiary or care manager rather than on the plans. Cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-06 for substantive structural-feature analysis.
*Evidence type:* [D] for parallel managed-care interface architecture.
*Confidence:* HIGH for structural feature.
*Representation implication:* PA-state-level engagement at OMHSAS cross-MCO coordination requirements; federal CMS engagement at Medicaid managed-care care-coordination standards and at MHPAEA enforcement.

**G21-SD6-06: PA-3 sub-area-disaggregated BH/SUD treatment utilization and infrastructure data gap.**
*Finding:* PA-3 sub-area-disaggregated BH/SUD treatment utilization, OTP geographic distribution, office-based buprenorphine prescriber concentration, inpatient psychiatric bed capacity, and CBH service utilization are not retrievable at four-sub-area resolution from public-facing data products. Per Standard 6 the limitation is stated; PA-3 magnitudes F-flagged (F21-SD6-01, F21-SD6-02, F21-SD6-05, F21-SD6-08).
*Evidence type:* [SI] for structural inference; [F] for sub-area magnitudes.
*Confidence:* MEDIUM.
*Representation implication:* Federal House representation engagement at SAMHSA data products; state engagement at CBH and DBHIDS data publication architecture.

## Section 8: Representation Question Closure

**What the legal framework provides or promises.** The federal behavioral health and SUD framework establishes: MHPAEA parity (29 U.S.C. § 1185a) between MH/SUD and medical/surgical benefits in commercial, ACA marketplace, Medicare Advantage, and Medicaid managed care plans; SAMHSA 42 C.F.R. Part 8 OTP regulatory architecture with permanent COVID-era flexibilities including telehealth and take-home doses; MAT Act DATA-Waiver elimination simplifying office-based buprenorphine prescribing; DEA-HHS permanent telemedicine flexibility for buprenorphine; 988 Suicide and Crisis Lifeline; 42 C.F.R. Part 2 / HIPAA harmonized SUD record confidentiality; CCBHC Medicaid demonstration; and OBBBA Sections 71107/71109/71115/71117/71119 Medicaid delivery-side flow-through (cross-reference D21 SD2). Pennsylvania's framework adds: PA Mental Health Procedures Act commitment architecture; PA Mental Health Parity Act (PA Act 50 of 2004) state-level commercial parity enforcement; PA Act 98 of 2022 telehealth permanence; PA Behavioral HealthChoices county-based BH-MCO carve-out architecture assigning Philadelphia exclusively to CBH; PA DDAP SUD facility licensing.

**What PA-3 constituents actually receive.** Approximately 420,000 Philadelphia Medicaid recipients receive BH coverage through CBH as the city-of-Philadelphia-contracted single BH-MCO under DBHIDS oversight [Source 87]; 100,700+ used CBH services in 2023 with $860,000,000+ total expenditures [Source 93]. CBH operates documented innovation architecture (school-based BH since 2003; CCBHC integration; D-SNP behavioral integration; CHC senior coordination team). PA-3 commercial-coverage enrollees receive MHPAEA-2013-baseline parity plus CAA 2021 NQTL comparative analysis requirements but face Trump-administration partial non-enforcement of 2024 Final Rule expansions for 2025 and 2026 plan years [Sources 94-98]; PA Insurance Department continues PA Mental Health Parity Act enforcement on PA-regulated commercial plans. PA-3 OUD patients receive substantively simplified MAT delivery through office-based buprenorphine prescribing (no DATA-Waiver per MAT Act; DEA Schedule III sufficient) with permanent telemedicine flexibility, plus OTP methadone delivery under 42 C.F.R. Part 8 permanent COVID-era flexibilities including take-home doses [Sources 88-90, 100-101]. PA-3 acute-crisis callers receive 988 routing to PA crisis-line architecture and mobile crisis coordination with CBH (Medicaid-enrolled) or DBHIDS (non-Medicaid). Cross-plan care coordination across CBH + PH-MCO + CHC-MCO + D-SNP interfaces operates with documented coordination burden on beneficiary or care manager (D21 SD2 G21-SD2-06).

**What accounts for the gap.** Five structural features account for the gap between framework and received delivery: (a) CBH single-MCO concentration risk (no inter-plan competition pressure; single-point-of-failure operational profile; OBBBA Section 71115/71117 fiscal-pressure flow-through) operationalizes the MC56 Both/And; (b) MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule partial non-enforcement disposition affects expanded parity protections (meaningful benefits standard; relevant data evaluation; discriminatory factors prohibition) at federal layer while 2013 baseline + state enforcement remains operative; (c) BH-PH care coordination architecture across four parallel managed-care interfaces (CBH + PH-MCO + CHC-MCO + D-SNP) places coordination burden on beneficiary; (d) PA-3 sub-area data gap at the four-sub-area resolution constrains evidence-based architectural-improvement targeting; (e) SAMHSA administrative posture under Trump HHS affects CCBHC expansion grants, targeted-population funding, harm-reduction-grant architecture, and culturally-responsive treatment funding — cross-reference D6 MC32 federal/state EJ-divergence context for the EJ-administrative-architecture-thinning flow-through to SAMHSA targeted-population funding. The cumulative effect: substantive federal SAMHSA + MAT Act architectural simplification operates positively at office-based buprenorphine layer; substantive CBH innovation architecture operates at Philadelphia Medicaid BH layer; structural MHPAEA enforcement vulnerability + CBH single-MCO concentration risk + BH-PH coordination burden + SAMHSA administrative vulnerability operate concurrently as structural-risk profile through 2026-2027.

**What the gap means for the representation analysis.** Federal House representation operates at: (a) MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule reconsideration oversight and statutory-amendment authority; (b) SAMHSA appropriation, CCBHC expansion grants, and 988 funding; (c) OBBBA technical corrections affecting BH-MCO capitation architecture (Sections 71115/71117 per D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02); (d) Medicaid managed-care oversight for cross-MCO coordination requirements; (e) DEA-HHS telemedicine flexibility implementation oversight. PA-state-level texture engagement at PA OMHSAS Behavioral HealthChoices contracting and rate-setting (CBH single-MCO capitation architecture); PA Insurance Department PA Mental Health Parity Act enforcement (state-enforcement growth per March 2026 Tri-Agencies report); PA DDAP SUD facility licensing; PA DBHIDS Philadelphia-level CBH oversight. The MC56 Both/And captures the central analytical posture: substantive CBH single-MCO architecture operates with documented innovation contribution AND structural single-MCO-concentration risk creates institutional-fragility profile distinct from competitive multi-MCO architectures. Federal House representation engagement on the upstream OBBBA Medicaid delivery-side flow-through (Sections 71107/71115/71117) directly affects CBH fiscal-pressure profile and downstream BH delivery capacity at the Philadelphia Medicaid layer.
