# D21 Healthcare Delivery — Sub-Domain 7
## Specialty Clinical and Cross-Cutting Delivery

**Date:** 2026-05-11
**Phase:** 1 (Per-SD Execution; fresh cycle)
**Domain:** 21 — Healthcare Delivery
**Sub-Domain:** 7 of 7
**Cycle position:** Phase 1 SD2-N batch — SD2-SD6 complete; SD7 final of batch
**Methodology stack:** M0 v2.2 · M2 v1.2.1 · Skills v3
**Status:** Phase 1 draft — SD7 of SD2-N batch (final SD of D21 Phase 1)

## Section 1: Header and Regime Declaration

**Sub-domain scope.** SD7 documents the specialty clinical and cross-cutting delivery architecture for PA-3 constituents — the residual sub-domain capturing service-lines, infrastructure layers, and architectural interfaces not owned by SD1-SD6. Specialty service-line coverage: oncology delivery (Penn Abramson Cancer Center; Temple cancer architecture; Jefferson Sidney Kimmel Cancer Center; CHOP pediatric oncology — substantive content cross-reference D2 SD7 cancer architecture); cardiology delivery (Penn Heart and Vascular; Temple Lung Center; Jefferson cardiology); orthopedic specialty; dialysis (ESRD architecture under Medicare Part B 100% coverage at qualifying ESRD providers; PA-3 dialysis center roster); pediatric specialty (CHOP centric architecture with 50+ Care Network locations [Source 58 SD4 cross-reference]). Pharmacy 340B delivery at PA-3 anchor hospitals (cross-reference D21 SD5 G21-SD5-01 + G21-SD5-02 — 340B Rebate Model Pilot Program court vacatur February 10, 2026 in *American Hospital Association v. Kennedy*, No. 25-cv-600 (D. Me.) [HRSA 340B page citation]; HRSA RFI comment period closed April 20, 2026; 2024 340B spending $66.3 billion with DSH hospitals at nearly $52 billion); Medicare telehealth flexibilities extended through December 31, 2027 under Consolidated Appropriations Act 2026 (P.L. 119-XX, signed February 3, 2026) plus CY 2026 Medicare Physician Fee Schedule Final Rule (CMS-1832-F, October 31, 2025) permanent direct supervision modifications and permanent behavioral-health audio-only telehealth flexibilities [Telehealth.hhs.gov; CMS Telehealth FAQ 2/26/26 citations]. Cross-cutting workforce architecture (physicians; nurses; PAs; pharmacists; behavioral health workforce; rural-vs-urban delivery; ACGME-funded GME pipeline per D21 SD1 G21-SD1-04 + SD4 G21-SD4-02). Title X family planning architecture (42 U.S.C. §§ 300 to 300a-6; OPA administration; $286.5 million FY 2025 + FY 2026 enacted appropriation; Pennsylvania is partially-affected state per Trump-administration March 2025 + March-April 2026 funding-disruption sequence [Guttmacher; NPR; Roll Call; KFF Title X Quick Take; CRS IF10051 citations]). 988 Suicide and Crisis Lifeline cross-cutting interface (substantive content cross-reference D21 SD6 G21-SD6-04). Workplace healthcare violence at PA-3 hospital EDs (cross-reference D10 SD3 OSHA architecture). BBP / HazCom institutional infection control (cross-reference D10 SD3). Medical respite + street medicine for homeless populations (CHOP medical respite; Penn/Drexel street medicine; cross-reference D7 SD6 + D2 homelessness architecture). School-based clinical delivery interface (cross-reference D11 SD7 SDP-Penn partnership; D11 SD3 SBHC where applicable; D6 MC-03 G6-SD4-02 SDP AHERA DOJ DPA PRINCIPAL ANCHOR for school-environmental-health intersection — first US school district criminally charged under AHERA; 5-year judicial monitoring; cross-reference for school-health intersection only, not re-extending substantive D6 content). Environmental health clinical interface (pediatric lead screening; CHOP environmental health clinic; cross-reference D6 SD2-4 + D13 SD2 LSL pediatric + AHERA respiratory). Veterans-civilian boundary (Veterans Choice / Community Care Network architecture; cross-reference D24 SD1 + SD4 for veterans health substantive territory). Institutional federal-contractor compliance (Section 503 + VEVRAA for healthcare-system employers; cross-reference D24 SD5 + D10 SD5).

**Three cross-domain principal-anchor deferrals operative across SD7** per kickoff §(3) directive — substantive content owned by D13 and D6 PRINCIPAL ANCHORS; SD7 cross-references with Standard 9 specificity and does NOT re-extend at framework level:

- **D13 SD7 G13-SD7-01 [D] HIGH — IIJA reauthorization September 30, 2026 inflection point PRINCIPAL ANCHOR.** IIJA (P.L. 117-58) expires September 30, 2026; no reauthorization bill introduced as of May 2026; congressional extensions expected mechanism if no reauthorization enacted by Oct 1, 2026 [D13 verified file]. House T&I Committee Chairman Sam Graves (R-MO) signaled "back to basics" approach; BASICS Act introduced February 9, 2026 by Reps. Rob Bresnahan (R-PA) and Kristen McDonald Rivet (D-MI) reflects ongoing legislative activity but is not the master reauthorization vehicle [D13 MC-02]. CRS R47573 (April 14, 2026) projects HTF balance at end of FY2026 ~$45 billion (~7 months avg outlays); CBO projects FTA may delay payments in Q3 FY2027; FHWA in Q2 FY2028 absent reauthorization or general fund transfers [CRS R47573 citation]. Transportation for America July 2025 analysis "Five Reasons Why IIJA Will Expire Without a Replacement in September 2026" identifies trust-fund-revenue gap and political-coalition fracture. D21 SD7 cross-references D13 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR for the IIJA reauthorization inflection-point architecture; D21 SD7 does NOT re-extend the substantive transportation-program analysis.

- **D13 SD4 G13-SD4-05 + D13 SD6 G13-SD6-05 + D13 SD2 G13-SD2-07 — Cumulative-burden geography Eastwick / Cobbs Creek PRINCIPAL ANCHOR.** D13 verified file documents Eastwick/Cobbs Creek geography intersects SD2 flood vulnerability + SD4 Lower Darby Creek Superfund adjacency + SD6 Heinz NWR adjacency; federal program architecture treats these as separate authorities (CWA NFIP/FEMA / CERCLA / FWS NWR System) while constituent experience is cumulative [D13 verified file]. Cross-reference for D21 SD7 healthcare-access-geography analysis at PA-3 sub-area resolution; D21 SD7 does NOT re-extend the substantive cumulative-burden analysis.

- **D13 PA-state-fiscal-architectural asymmetry — PRINCIPAL ANCHOR.** D13 verified file documents PA-state-fiscal-architectural asymmetry between PA-3 federal-program flow-through and state-level fiscal capacity. Cross-reference for D21 SD7 healthcare-financing analysis at PA state level (PA Medicaid PPS rate-setting; PA Hospital and Healthsystem Association projections under OBBBA + EPTC-expiration flow-through; cross-reference D9 SD4 PILOET fiscal-anchor analysis); D21 SD7 does NOT re-extend.

**Regime declaration.** Internet-access regime. Current-state IIJA reauthorization status, Medicare telehealth flexibility extensions through December 31, 2027, 340B Rebate Pilot court vacatur disposition (February 10, 2026 *AHA v. Kennedy* D. Me.), Title X funding disposition under Trump-administration 2025-2026 actions, FY 2026 Medicare Physician Fee Schedule Final Rule (October 31, 2025), and OBBBA Section 71401 Rural Health Transformation Fund implementation are expected to be retrieved; specific figures carry sources per Standard 12. Cross-domain principal-anchor content at D13 IIJA + cumulative-burden + PA-fiscal-asymmetry and D6 MC-03 AHERA G6-SD4-02 + MC-04/32 EJ-divergence is inherited as ESTABLISHED CONTEXT and not re-extended. Mixed-regime drafting prohibited per Standard 1.

**Analytical posture.** Multiple Both/And designations operative at SD7 reflecting the 10+ domain-interface scope:

- **MC57 candidate Both/And (IIJA reauthorization inflection-point delivery-side flow-through).** Per D13 G13-SD7-01 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR cross-reference, IIJA reauthorization September 30, 2026 deadline operates as inflection point affecting healthcare-delivery-adjacent infrastructure (FTA transit access to PA-3 healthcare facilities including academic medical centers; PennDOT/FHWA road infrastructure affecting EMS and patient access; DCNR LWCF parks-and-recreation infrastructure affecting community health architectures cross-reference D13 SD6). Substantive IIJA-funded infrastructure delivery continues operative through September 30, 2026 AND structural federal-architecture uncertainty at reauthorization trajectory creates planning-horizon disruption for healthcare-delivery-adjacent infrastructure. MC57 candidate surfaced for batch checkpoint review; lead direction pending on formalization.

- **MC58 candidate Both/And (340B Rebate Model Pilot court vacatur post-RFI trajectory).** Per HRSA 340B page citation, the February 10, 2026 *AHA v. Kennedy* (D. Me., No. 25-cv-600) decision vacated and remanded to HHS the 340B Rebate Model Pilot Program Application Notice (August 1, 2025) + Corrected Application Notice (August 7, 2025) + approvals of manufacturer applications (announced October 30 - November 14, 2025). HRSA RFI on potential rebate model use closed April 20, 2026 [HRSA citation]. Substantive 340B upfront-discount architecture continues operative at PA-3 anchor hospitals (Penn Medicine, Temple, Jefferson, CHOP qualifying as DSH 340B entities — 2024 340B spending $66.3B with DSH at $52B) AND structural HRSA post-RFI rulemaking trajectory + HHS FY 2026 proposed move of 340B oversight from HRSA to CMS creates regulatory-architecture uncertainty. MC58 candidate surfaced for batch checkpoint review.

- **MC59 candidate Both/And (Title X family planning Trump-administration disruption + Pennsylvania partial-affected exposure).** Title X $286.5M FY 2025 + FY 2026 enacted appropriation continues operative at statutory level (PHSA 42 U.S.C. §§ 300 to 300a-6; Trump FY 2026 budget request to eliminate Title X did NOT prevail; Congress included Title X funding in 2026 appropriation [CRS IF10051; KFF; Roll Call citations]) AND structural Trump-administration administrative-disruption disposition through March-April 2025 funding-withholding sequence (16 grantees / 22 grants / ~$65.8M withheld; ~842,000 patients affected; Pennsylvania named partial-affected state [Guttmacher citation]); December 2025 restoration of year-4 funds after NFPRHA lawsuit; March 13, 2026 HHS guidance with 1-week response window removing "Quality Family Planning" standards and equity/inclusion programmatic goals; April 1, 2026 Trump-administration FY 2026 grants including to Planned Parenthood affiliates [Roll Call; KFF; NPR citations]. MC59 candidate surfaced for batch checkpoint review.

- **Telehealth Both/And (post-PHE flexibility extensions through December 31, 2027).** Medicare telehealth flexibilities extended through December 31, 2027 under Consolidated Appropriations Act 2026 (signed February 3, 2026) [Telehealth.org; Telehealth.hhs.gov; Lamb McErlane citations]; CY 2026 PFS Final Rule (October 31, 2025; CMS-1832-F) finalized permanent direct supervision via real-time two-way audio-visual telecommunications; permanent virtual teaching physician presence; behavioral/mental health in-person visit requirement waived through December 31, 2027; non-behavioral audio-only telehealth through December 31, 2027 [CMS Telehealth FAQ 2/26/26 citation]. Substantive telehealth-access architecture continues operative through 2027 AND structural reversion-to-pre-PHE-architecture risk operates after December 31, 2027 absent further Congressional action; advocacy for permanent telehealth legislation continues [Telehealth.org citation].

**Hold-open-magnitude territory** — none new operative at SD7; HOM project-wide instances concentrate at SD4 (commitment-vs-outcome HOM 7th-instance candidate at G21-SD4-01) and SD5 (emergent-from-interaction HOM 2nd-instance candidate at G21-SD5-01). The MC55 SD5 emergent-from-interaction-shape cumulative-architecture analytical structure operates as cross-reference context at SD7 for the convergence of IIJA reauthorization + Medicare telehealth post-2027 reversion + 340B post-RFI trajectory + Title X administrative disruption + OBBBA flow-through (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02; D21 SD5 G21-SD5-01) — six concurrent federal-policy-cycle mechanisms with their own intended effects, none intending cumulative outcome on PA-3 healthcare-delivery infrastructure.

**Thread declarations.** Four analytical threads operative at SD7 (with high cross-cutting reach per kickoff §(3) directive):

- **D21-SD7-Thread A (cross-domain principal-anchor deferral architecture).** D13 SD7 G13-SD7-01 IIJA reauthorization, D13 G13-SD4-05 + G13-SD6-05 + G13-SD2-07 cumulative-burden geography, D13 PA-state-fiscal-architectural asymmetry, D6 MC-03 G6-SD4-02 SDP AHERA DOJ DPA, D6 MC-04 RISE PA industrial decarbonization, D6 MC32 federal/state EJ-divergence, D2 SD7 cancer architecture, D9 SD4 PILOET fiscal-anchor, D9 SD7 federal/state tax burden distribution, D10 healthcare workforce architecture, D11 SD7 SDP-Penn partnership, D24 SD1/SD4/SD5 veterans-civilian boundary architecture are PRINCIPAL ANCHORS for substantive content owned by upstream domains. D21 SD7 cross-references each with Standard 9 specificity; does NOT re-extend substantive content; integrates at the healthcare-delivery-architecture interface only.

- **D21-SD7-Thread B (specialty service-line architecture at PA-3 anchor institutions).** Oncology delivery at Penn Abramson Cancer Center, Temple, Jefferson Sidney Kimmel, CHOP pediatric oncology (cross-reference D2 SD7 substantive cancer architecture); cardiology delivery at academic medical centers; orthopedic specialty; ESRD/dialysis delivery under Medicare Part B 100% coverage architecture; specialty-care concentration at PA-3 anchor institutions affecting sub-area access geography (cross-reference D21 SD4 hospital institutional architecture; D21 SD5 FQHC + safety-net delivery; D13 cumulative-burden geography concentration in North/Northwest Core + West Philadelphia Core + South/Southwest sub-areas).

- **D21-SD7-Thread C (cross-cutting workforce architecture; ACGME-funded GME pipeline; specialty-distribution patterns).** ACGME-accredited residency and fellowship training programs at Penn, Temple, Jefferson, CHOP collectively operate the substantial majority of PA-3 GME training capacity (D21 SD4 G21-SD4-02). Medicare GME funding flows through hospital cost reports per Medicare GME architecture (D21 SD1 GME territory). Specialty-distribution patterns: PA-3 specialty-care concentration at the four academic medical centers with documented primary-care workforce gap downstream of specialty-selection economics (D21 SD4 G21-SD4-02). Nursing workforce architecture; pharmacist workforce architecture; PA / NP advanced-practice workforce architecture; behavioral-health workforce architecture (cross-reference D21 SD6 G21-SD6-03 MAT Act simplification + DEA-HHS permanent telemedicine flexibility addressing workforce-access bottleneck).

- **D21-SD7-Thread D (cross-cutting federal-policy-cycle convergence: IIJA + Medicare telehealth post-2027 + 340B post-RFI + Title X disruption + OBBBA flow-through; SD7 closes Phase 1 architecturally at the convergence layer).** Six concurrent federal-policy-cycle mechanisms operating against PA-3 healthcare-delivery infrastructure through 2026-2027: (a) IIJA reauthorization September 30, 2026 inflection point affecting healthcare-delivery-adjacent infrastructure (cross-reference D13 G13-SD7-01); (b) Medicare telehealth post-2027 reversion risk after December 31, 2027 (cross-reference Telehealth.hhs.gov citations); (c) 340B Rebate Model Pilot post-RFI HRSA rulemaking trajectory (cross-reference D21 SD5 G21-SD5-02; HRSA citation); (d) Title X administrative-disruption disposition under Trump HHS (cross-reference KFF; NPR; Roll Call citations); (e) OBBBA Sections 71107/71109/71115/71117/71119/71401 Medicaid delivery-side flow-through and Rural Health Transformation Fund (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02; D21 SD5 G21-SD5-01); (f) MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule Trump-administration partial non-enforcement (cross-reference D21 SD6 G21-SD6-02). Each mechanism has own intended effect; none intends cumulative healthcare-delivery-architecture-fragility outcome. SD7 closes Phase 1 architecturally at this convergence-layer where the per-SD load-bearing findings cohere.

## Section 2: Authority Chain

The specialty clinical and cross-cutting delivery authority chain in PA-3 reflects the high cross-cutting reach of SD7 — substantial cross-references to prior SD architectures rather than substantial new statutory citations.

**Constitutional Foundation.** U.S. Const. Art. I § 8 (Commerce Clause; Spending Clause; General Welfare Clause) grounds federal Medicare/Medicaid, 340B, Title X, Medicare Part B ESRD, IIJA, and Medicare telehealth architecture; Tenth Amendment reserves state regulatory authority for SUD, BH, hospital licensure, healthcare workforce licensure, and pharmacy regulation; 14th Amendment Equal Protection grounding for Title VI healthcare access analysis. *NFIB v. Sebelius*, *King v. Burwell*, and *California v. Texas* preserved ACA architecture; cross-reference D21 SD3 G21-SD3-04 ERISA preemption architecture.

**Federal Statutory Layer.**

- **IIJA (P.L. 117-58; Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act / "Bipartisan Infrastructure Law").** Signed November 15, 2021; authorized $1.2 trillion total / $550B new investment over five years FY2022-FY2026; **expires September 30, 2026** [CRS R47573; CRS R48644; Funding Landscape citations]. Substantive authority across IIJA delivery-side flow-through to healthcare-delivery-adjacent infrastructure deferred to D13 SD7 G13-SD7-01 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR. *Statutory stability HIGH for current authorization period; trajectory uncertain post-September 30, 2026 per D13 MC-02. Administrative vulnerability HIGH on reauthorization outcome.*

- **Medicare Telehealth Statutory Architecture (Section 1834(m) of the Social Security Act).** Authorizes Medicare telehealth payment architecture; CARES Act (P.L. 116-136) + CAA 2021 + CAA 2023 + CAA 2024 + continuing resolutions + **Consolidated Appropriations Act 2026 (signed February 3, 2026) extending Medicare telehealth flexibilities through December 31, 2027** [Telehealth.org; Telehealth.hhs.gov citations]. CY 2026 Medicare Physician Fee Schedule Final Rule (October 31, 2025; CMS-1832-F) permanent direct supervision via real-time two-way audio-visual telecommunications; permanent virtual teaching physician presence; permanent removal of telehealth frequency limits on subsequent inpatient and nursing facility visits; behavioral/mental health in-person visit requirement waived through December 31, 2027 (and grandfathered for beneficiaries who began services on or before January 30, 2026) [CMS Telehealth FAQ 2/26/26 citation; Quarles Law; Psychiatry.org citations]. *Statutory stability MODERATE-HIGH at current extensions; administrative vulnerability HIGH at December 31, 2027 reversion risk absent further Congressional action.*

- **340B Drug Pricing Program (42 U.S.C. § 256b).** Substantive 340B architecture cross-reference D21 SD5 G21-SD5-01 + G21-SD5-02. SD7-specific surface: 340B at PA-3 anchor hospitals operating as DSH 340B-qualifying entities (Penn Medicine, Temple, Jefferson, CHOP — DSH eligibility requires owned/operated/contracted by state/local government plus disproportionate-share adjustment percentage of 11.75% or greater per CRS R48696 [Congressional Research Service citation]). **February 10, 2026 *AHA v. Kennedy* (D. Me., No. 25-cv-600)** vacated and remanded to HHS the 340B Rebate Model Pilot Program Application Notice (August 1, 2025; 90 Fed. Reg. 36,163), Corrected Application Notice (August 7, 2025; 90 Fed. Reg. 38,165), and manufacturer-application approvals (October 30 - November 14, 2025) [HRSA 340B page citation]. HRSA RFI on rebate models closed April 20, 2026. 2024 340B spending: $66.3 billion; DSH hospitals account for nearly $52 billion [340B Wikipedia citation]. HHS FY 2026 proposed budget would move 340B oversight from HRSA to CMS [Commonwealth Fund citation]. *Statutory stability HIGH; administrative vulnerability HIGH at HRSA post-RFI rulemaking + potential HHS reorganization.*

- **Title X (Public Health Service Act, 42 U.S.C. §§ 300 to 300a-6).** Authorized 1970; HHS Office of Population Affairs (OPA) administration; 42 C.F.R. Part 59 regulations. FY 2025: $286.5 million enacted per P.L. 119-4; **FY 2026: $286 million enacted (signed by President despite Trump FY 2026 budget request to eliminate)** [CRS IF10051; KFF citations]. 3,853 Title X clinics as of 2023 serving 2.8 million people. **2025-2026 Trump-administration administrative-disruption sequence:** March 31, 2025 withholding of 16 grantees / 22 grants / $65.8M / ~842,000 patients affected including Pennsylvania [Guttmacher; CRS IF10051 citations]; December 2025 restoration of year-4 funds after NFPRHA lawsuit; March 13, 2026 HHS guidance with one-week response window removing "Quality Family Planning" standards and equity/inclusion programmatic goals [KFF; NPR; Health Law Policy Brief citations]; April 1, 2026 Trump-administration FY 2026 grant awards including to Planned Parenthood affiliates [Roll Call citation]. *Statutory stability HIGH at appropriation; administrative vulnerability HIGH at HHS implementation and at potential reinstatement of 2019 Protect Life Rule.*

- **Medicare Part B ESRD coverage (42 U.S.C. § 1395rr).** 100% coverage for ESRD beneficiaries regardless of age after qualifying period; PA-3 dialysis center delivery architecture operates under Medicare Part B fee-for-service or Medicare Advantage ESRD enrollment (cross-reference D21 SD1 MA architecture).

- **OBBBA Section 71401 Rural Health Transformation Fund (RHTF).** $50B total / $10B annually 2026-2030; rural-only architecture; does not directly serve urban PA-3 healthcare-delivery infrastructure [Feldesman D21 SD5 Source 74 cross-reference; D21 SD5 G21-SD5-01]. First distributions in 2026; PA share and PA-3-relevant institutional flow-through F-flagged (cross-reference D21 SD5 F21-SD5-07).

- **Veterans Choice / Community Care Network (CCN).** 38 U.S.C. § 1703 architecture (MISSION Act of 2018; CCN 2.0 architecture). Substantive content cross-reference D24 SD1 + SD4 veterans health architecture.

- **Section 503 Rehabilitation Act + VEVRAA federal-contractor compliance.** Cross-reference D24 SD5 + D10 SD5 substantive content.

- **OSHA Bloodborne Pathogens Standard (29 C.F.R. § 1910.1030) + Hazard Communication Standard (29 C.F.R. § 1910.1200) + workplace violence guidance.** Cross-reference D10 SD3 substantive content.

- **AHERA / SDP DPA (cross-reference D6 MC-03 G6-SD4-02 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR).** DOJ DPA filed June 26, 2025; 8 counts; 31 SDP buildings 2015-2023; 5-year judicial monitoring; first US school district criminally charged under AHERA; Penn $100M contribution to SDP environmental management; Frankford High closed [D6 verified file MC-03 citation]. School-environmental-health intersection at PA-3 pediatric specialty (CHOP environmental health clinic; Penn Medicine pediatric environmental); D21 SD7 cross-reference for school-health intersection only.

- **Title VI (42 U.S.C. § 2000d).** Cross-reference D13 SD7 G13-SD7-02 / G13-SD2-07 Justice40 architecture revocation context per D6 G6-SD5-01.

- **One Big Beautiful Bill Act of 2025 (P.L. 119-21) cross-cutting provisions.** Cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02 (Sections 71107/71109/71115/71117/71119); D21 SD5 G21-SD5-04 (Section 71109 noncitizen Medicaid restrictions October 1, 2026); D21 SD5 <a id="g21-sd5-07"></a>G21-SD5-07 (Section 71401 RHTF).

**Federal Agency Layer.**

- **Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS).** Administers Medicare Part B specialty service-line architecture; ESRD coverage; Medicare telehealth implementation per CY 2026 PFS Final Rule; Medicare Advantage telehealth coverage architecture. *Administrative vulnerability HIGH at Trump-administration CMS posture* [cross-reference D21 SD1 Source 14 EO 14273 pattern].

- **Health Resources and Services Administration (HRSA), Office of Pharmacy Affairs.** 5600 Fishers Lane, 08W05A, Rockville, MD 20857. Administers 340B Drug Pricing Program; pending HHS FY 2026 reorganization to move 340B to CMS [Commonwealth Fund citation]. *Administrative vulnerability HIGH*.

- **HHS Office of Population Affairs (OPA).** Administers Title X family planning architecture under PHSA. *per Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out: National Family Planning and Reproductive Health Association (NFPRHA) President Clare Coleman identified per source-grounded NPR, Roll Call, KFF, and Health Law Policy Brief citations as advocacy-organization principal* (Coleman is not a federal official; identification reflects source-grounded advocacy-organization-leader context per Standard 13.C carve-out applicability to source-grounded named individuals in publicly-documented capacity). *per Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out: HHS Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. identified in official capacity per source-grounded NPR citation and Roll Call citation*. *Administrative vulnerability HIGH at Trump HHS posture on Title X reinstatement of 2019 Protect Life Rule and Project 2025 reorientation*.

- **USDOT Federal Transit Administration (FTA) + Federal Highway Administration (FHWA).** Cross-reference D13 SD7 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR for substantive IIJA architecture. *Administrative vulnerability HIGH at Trump USDOT posture per D13 MC-02 + MC-03*.

- **HHS Office for Civil Rights (OCR).** Administers Title VI healthcare-access oversight; cross-reference D6 G6-SD5-01 + D13 G13-SD2-07 Justice40 architecture revocation context. *Administrative vulnerability HIGH*.

- **U.S. Department of Veterans Affairs (VA) + DOJ Office of Federal Contract Compliance Programs (OFCCP).** Cross-reference D24 + D10 substantive content.

- **OSHA.** Cross-reference D10 SD3 substantive content.

- **ACGME.** Cross-reference D21 SD4 G21-SD4-02.

**State Statutory Layer.**

- **PA Health Care Facilities Act (35 P.S. § 448.101 et seq.).** Cross-reference D21 SD4 substantive content for hospital licensure architecture.

- **PA Public Welfare Code (62 P.S. § 101 et seq.).** Cross-reference D21 SD2 substantive content for PA Medicaid PPS architecture affecting specialty service-line reimbursement.

- **PA MCARE Act (40 P.S. § 1303.101 et seq.).** Cross-reference D21 SD4 G21-SD4-05 medical malpractice + 2024 tort reform architecture.

- **PA Healthcare Workforce Licensure (multiple titles).** PA Department of State Bureau of Professional and Occupational Affairs administers physician, nurse, PA, pharmacist licensure architecture.

**State Agency Layer.**

- **PA DHS + PA DOH + PA Insurance Department + PA DDAP.** Cross-reference D21 SD1-SD6 substantive content.

- **PA Hospital and Healthsystem Association (HAP).** Industry association representing PA hospitals; advocacy on OBBBA implementation, Medicare reimbursement, and 340B architecture. PA-3 anchor hospital institutional advocacy interface.

**Local Statutory Layer.** Philadelphia Code Title 6 (Health); cross-reference D21 SD2 + SD4 substantive content.

**Local Agency Layer.**

- **Philadelphia DPH (PDPH); DBHIDS; Philadelphia County Assistance Office (CAO).** Cross-reference D21 SD2-SD6 substantive content.

- **School District of Philadelphia (SDP).** Cross-reference D11 SD7 SDP-Penn partnership + D6 MC-03 G6-SD4-02 SDP AHERA DOJ DPA PRINCIPAL ANCHOR; SD7 cross-reference for school-environmental-health pediatric specialty intersection only.

- **PA-3 specialty clinical infrastructure.** Penn Medicine specialty (Abramson Cancer Center; Penn Heart and Vascular Center; Penn neurology; Penn dialysis at HUP and Penn Presbyterian; specialty surgical); Temple Health specialty (Temple Lung Center; cardiology; specialty surgical); Jefferson Health specialty (Sidney Kimmel Cancer Center; Vickie and Jack Farber Institute for Neuroscience; specialty surgical; Methodist trauma center per D21 SD4 Profile 4); CHOP pediatric specialty across all subspecialties plus 50+ Care Network locations; Wills Eye Hospital ophthalmology specialty; St. Christopher's Hospital for Children pediatric specialty (Tower Health-owned). PA-3 dialysis center roster F-flagged (F21-SD7-01).

**Constituent Layer.** Aggregate constituent profiles introduced in Section 5: PA-3 patient navigating oncology specialty at Penn Abramson plus 340B pharmacy interface (post-court-vacatur HRSA RFI architecture); PA-3 ESRD patient navigating dialysis delivery under Medicare Part B; PA-3 Title X-served patient navigating Pennsylvania partial-affected funding-disruption sequence; PA-3 telehealth user navigating Medicare-extended-through-2027 architecture plus state-level Medicaid telehealth alignment.

## Section 3: Pathway Tracing

The specialty clinical and cross-cutting delivery pathway for a PA-3 constituent depends on service line (oncology / cardiology / orthopedic / ESRD-dialysis / pediatric specialty / reproductive health / SUD specialty), insurance type (Medicare / Medicare Advantage / Medicaid HealthChoices / commercial / dual-eligible / uninsured), institutional access (academic medical center / community hospital / FQHC / Title X clinic / dialysis center / Veterans Health Administration), and federal-policy-cycle layer (IIJA-adjacent infrastructure / 340B-architected pharmacy / Medicare-telehealth-extended / Title X-funded reproductive health). D21 SD7 traces these pathways with cross-domain principal-anchor deferrals operative at D13 (IIJA + cumulative-burden + PA-fiscal-asymmetry) and D6 (MC-03 AHERA + MC-04/32 EJ).

*Triggering condition.* A PA-3 constituent enters specialty/cross-cutting delivery architecture under documented pathways: (a) primary care referral to specialty (oncology, cardiology, orthopedic, neurology, GI, pediatric subspecialty) following diagnostic workup at primary care or FQHC; (b) ESRD diagnosis triggering Medicare Part B 100% coverage qualifying period and dialysis-center delivery initiation; (c) Title X clinic visit for reproductive health (contraception, STI testing, cancer screening, wellness check); (d) telehealth visit for primary care, specialty consultation, behavioral health, or SUD follow-up under Medicare-extended-through-2027 + Medicaid PA Act 98 telehealth architecture; (e) IIJA-funded infrastructure-adjacent healthcare access (SEPTA transit to academic medical centers; FHWA road infrastructure affecting EMS routing; cross-reference D13 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR); (f) school-based environmental-health intersection at pediatric specialty (CHOP environmental health clinic; Penn pediatric environmental; cross-reference D6 MC-03 G6-SD4-02 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR for substantive AHERA-criminal-enforcement context); (g) veterans-civilian boundary triggering Veterans Choice / Community Care Network architecture (cross-reference D24 SD1 + SD4); (h) homeless-population specialty access via medical respite / street medicine (CHOP medical respite; Penn / Drexel street medicine; cross-reference D7 SD6).

*Specialty service-line pathway at PA-3 anchor institutions.* PA-3 constituent referred to oncology presents at Penn Abramson Cancer Center, Temple cancer architecture, Jefferson Sidney Kimmel Cancer Center, or CHOP pediatric oncology (cross-reference D2 SD7 cancer substantive architecture). Cardiology referral routes to Penn Heart and Vascular, Temple Lung Center / cardiology, or Jefferson cardiology. Orthopedic and specialty surgical referrals route across PA-3 academic medical centers. The substantive specialty content architecture (treatment protocols; clinical outcomes; specialty workforce capacity) is owned by D2 + D21 SD4 + Domain-specific D2 SD7 cancer architecture. D21 SD7 documents the cross-cutting delivery-side architecture: prior-authorization architecture at PH-MCO (Medicaid) or Medicare Advantage or commercial; specialty-network adequacy at PH-MCO and CHC-MCO (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-05); 340B pharmacy interface for specialty pharmacotherapy (oncology-administered drugs constitute substantial portion of 340B oncology spending per Wikipedia and Medicine to Market citations); telehealth specialty consultation under Medicare-extended-through-2027 architecture.

*340B pharmacy delivery pathway at PA-3 anchor hospitals (cross-reference D21 SD5 G21-SD5-01 + G21-SD5-02).* PA-3 anchor hospitals (Penn, Temple, Jefferson, CHOP) operating as DSH 340B-qualifying entities purchase 340B-discounted drugs from manufacturers at typically 20-50% below standard wholesale acquisition cost [340B Wikipedia; Medicine to Market citations]; the spread between 340B pricing and standard insurance reimbursement generates revenue used to cross-subsidize teaching, research, and uncompensated care [Daily Remedy 340B Coalition citation]. **February 10, 2026 *AHA v. Kennedy* (D. Me., No. 25-cv-600)** vacated and remanded to HHS the 340B Rebate Model Pilot Program Application Notice (90 Fed. Reg. 36,163; August 1, 2025), Corrected Application Notice (90 Fed. Reg. 38,165; August 7, 2025), and the manufacturer-application approvals (announced October 30 - November 14, 2025) [HRSA 340B page citation]. HRSA RFI on potential rebate model use closed April 20, 2026. HHS FY 2026 proposed budget would move 340B oversight from HRSA to CMS [Commonwealth Fund citation]. Contract pharmacy architecture: 32,000+ contract pharmacy arrangements (2024) up from a few hundred before 2010 HRSA guidance permitting unlimited contract pharmacies [Daily Remedy citation]; manufacturer-imposed contract pharmacy restrictions remain in legal contestation [AHA citation]. PA-3 anchor hospital 340B participation generates documented operating-margin contribution; the post-court-vacatur HRSA RFI rulemaking trajectory is the principal forward-trajectory variable.

*Medicare telehealth pathway (extended through December 31, 2027).* PA-3 Medicare beneficiary receives telehealth services under Section 1834(m) statutory architecture extended through December 31, 2027 by Consolidated Appropriations Act 2026 (signed February 3, 2026) [Telehealth.org; Telehealth.hhs.gov citations]: (a) home-based telehealth without geographic restriction; (b) audio-only non-behavioral telehealth; (c) FQHCs and RHCs as distant-site providers via HCPCS code G2025; (d) expanded distant-site practitioner list including physical therapists, occupational therapists, speech-language pathologists, audiologists, marriage and family therapists, mental health counselors; (e) behavioral/mental telehealth without in-person visit requirement (grandfather provision for beneficiaries who began services on or before January 30, 2026; subsequent annual in-person required after 12/31/27 unless extended) [CMS Telehealth FAQ 2/26/26 citation]. CY 2026 PFS Final Rule (CMS-1832-F, October 31, 2025) permanent changes: (a) "direct supervision" via real-time two-way audio-visual telecommunications; (b) virtual teaching physician presence; (c) permanent removal of telehealth frequency limits on subsequent inpatient and nursing facility visits [Quarles; Psychiatry.org citations]. **Audio-only for behavioral/mental telehealth is permanent under certain conditions** [Telehealth.hhs.gov citation]; this is one of the few areas where post-PHE permanent statutory architecture is operative. Pennsylvania state-level: PA Act 98 of 2022 permits audio-only telehealth in PA outpatient psychiatric and SUD clinics (cross-reference D21 SD6 Thread A); Pennsylvania Medicaid coverage of telehealth operates under PA DHS implementation (state Medicaid telehealth coverage rules vary by state and are not automatically tied to Medicare extensions per CCHP citation).

*Title X family planning pathway under Trump-administration disruption sequence.* PA-3 constituent seeking reproductive health services at a Title X-funded clinic encounters the 2025-2026 funding-disruption sequence: (a) March 31, 2025 administration withholding of 16 grantees / 22 Title X grants / ~$65.8M affecting ~842,000 patients nationally; Pennsylvania named among partially-affected states [Guttmacher; CRS IF10051 citations]; (b) NFPRHA lawsuit filed April 24, 2025 in U.S. District Court for the District of Columbia [CRS IF10051 citation]; (c) December 2025 HHS restoration of year-4 funds; (d) March 13, 2026 HHS issuance of new Title X guidance with one-week response window (March 13-20) removing "Quality Family Planning" standards and equity/inclusion programmatic goals [KFF; NPR; Health Law Policy Brief citations]; (e) April 1, 2026 Trump-administration FY 2026 Title X grants announced including to Planned Parenthood affiliates, sparking anti-abortion-group backlash [Roll Call citation]; (f) potential reinstatement of 2019 "Protect Life Rule" remains pending per HHS notice of proposed rulemaking signaled in spring 2025 [Roll Call citation]. PA-3 Title X-served patients access contraception, STI testing/treatment, cancer screening, wellness checks at Title X clinics; the administrative-disruption sequence affects clinic operational stability without altering the FY 2026 $286M appropriated funding floor.

*IIJA-funded healthcare-adjacent infrastructure pathway (cross-reference D13 G13-SD7-01 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR).* PA-3 constituent's access to specialty care depends on IIJA-funded transit and road infrastructure including SEPTA transit to academic medical centers, FHWA road infrastructure affecting EMS routing, and IIJA-funded community-health-supportive infrastructure. The substantive transportation-program architecture is owned by D13 SD7 G13-SD7-01 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR; SD7 cross-references at the healthcare-delivery-access interface. The September 30, 2026 IIJA expiration inflection point creates planning-horizon disruption for healthcare-adjacent infrastructure absent reauthorization or extension [CRS R47573 citation projects HTF balance ~$45B at end of FY2026 = ~7 months avg outlays; FTA may delay payments Q3 FY2027; FHWA Q2 FY2028]. No reauthorization bill introduced as of May 2026 [Funding Landscape; T4America citations]; congressional extensions expected mechanism.

*Cumulative-burden geography healthcare-access pathway (cross-reference D13 G13-SD4-05 + G13-SD6-05 + G13-SD2-07 PRINCIPAL ANCHORS).* PA-3 sub-area healthcare access is structurally shaped by D13-documented cumulative-burden geography: Eastwick / Cobbs Creek geography intersects SD2 flood vulnerability + SD4 Lower Darby Creek Superfund adjacency + SD6 Heinz NWR boundary-adjacency; constituent experience is cumulative across federal-program authority lines [D13 verified file]. The healthcare-access dimension: PA-3 South/Southwest sub-area (encompassing Eastwick) faces compound environmental-burden plus healthcare-infrastructure-access patterns; North/Northwest Core and West Philadelphia Core sub-areas face concentrated equity-gap patterns per D13 cumulative-burden geography. D21 SD7 cross-references at the healthcare-access-geography interface; D21 SD7 does NOT re-extend the substantive cumulative-burden analysis owned by D13.

*Workforce pipeline pathway (ACGME GME architecture + Medicare GME funding + specialty-distribution patterns).* PA-3 specialty workforce pipeline operates through ACGME-accredited residency and fellowship training at Penn, Temple, Jefferson, CHOP (cross-reference D21 SD4 G21-SD4-02). Medicare GME funding flows through hospital cost reports (cross-reference D21 SD1 GME territory). Specialty-distribution downstream: PA-3 specialty-care concentration at the four academic medical centers operates alongside documented primary-care workforce gap (D21 SD4 G21-SD4-02); MAT Act + DEA-HHS permanent telemedicine flexibility for buprenorphine prescribing has addressed the principal SUD workforce-access bottleneck (cross-reference D21 SD6 G21-SD6-03).

*Veterans-civilian boundary pathway (cross-reference D24 SD1 + SD4).* PA-3 veteran constituent accesses VA medical care directly through VA Philadelphia Medical Center or through Veterans Choice / Community Care Network architecture under 38 U.S.C. § 1703 (MISSION Act of 2018) for community-provider-furnished care. Substantive content cross-reference D24 verified file.

*School-based clinical delivery + environmental-health pathway (cross-reference D11 SD7 + D6 MC-03 G6-SD4-02 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR).* PA-3 SDP student accesses school-based clinical services through SBHC architecture (cross-reference D11 SD3 where applicable) and through CBH school-based BH program (cross-reference D21 SD6 Profile 3). School-environmental-health intersection at AHERA-implicated buildings: CHOP environmental health clinic and Penn Medicine pediatric environmental specialty receive referrals from SDP students with environmental-trigger asthma, lead exposure, and respiratory disease — the D6 MC-03 G6-SD4-02 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR documents the SDP AHERA DOJ DPA architecture; D21 SD7 cross-references at the pediatric specialty intersection only.

*Decision points where the pathway breaks for PA-3 constituents.* The specialty/cross-cutting pathway breaks down at: (a) specialty-network adequacy under PA Medicaid PH-MCO / CHC-MCO (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-05); (b) 340B Rebate Model Pilot post-RFI rulemaking trajectory (HRSA + potential HHS reorganization to CMS) affecting PA-3 anchor hospital pharmacy revenue architecture; (c) Title X 2025-2026 administrative-disruption sequence creating operational instability at PA-3-serving Title X clinics; (d) Medicare telehealth post-December 31, 2027 reversion risk absent further Congressional action; (e) IIJA reauthorization September 30, 2026 inflection-point creating planning-horizon disruption for healthcare-delivery-adjacent infrastructure (cross-reference D13 G13-SD7-01); (f) ESRD dialysis delivery dependent on Medicare Part B continuity + dialysis-center geographic distribution; (g) cumulative-burden geography concentration in Eastwick / Cobbs Creek / North-Northwest Core / West Philadelphia Core compounds healthcare-access disparities (cross-reference D13 PRINCIPAL ANCHORS); (h) workforce-pipeline gaps in primary care, behavioral health, and pediatric specialty (cross-reference D21 SD4 G21-SD4-02); (i) homeless-population specialty access dependent on medical respite + street medicine program capacity (cross-reference D7 SD6); (j) AHERA-implicated school-environmental-health intersection at pediatric specialty (cross-reference D6 MC-03 G6-SD4-02 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR).

## Section 4: Statistical Profile and Sub-Area Variation

**PA-3 specialty institutional roster (operating context cross-reference D21 SD4).** Penn Medicine specialty: Hospital of the University of Pennsylvania (3400 Spruce Street); Penn Presbyterian Medical Center (51 N. 39th Street); Pennsylvania Hospital (800 Spruce Street); Abramson Cancer Center (specialty oncology); Penn Heart and Vascular Center (specialty cardiology). Temple Health specialty: Temple University Hospital (3401 N. Broad Street); Episcopal Campus (100 E. Lehigh Avenue); Temple Lung Center; cardiology. Jefferson Health: Thomas Jefferson University Hospitals (111 S. 11th Street); Einstein Medical Center (5501 Old York Road); Jefferson Methodist (2301 S. Broad Street); Sidney Kimmel Cancer Center; Vickie and Jack Farber Institute for Neuroscience. CHOP: 3401 Civic Center Boulevard plus 50+ Care Network locations across PA and NJ (cross-reference D21 SD4). Wills Eye Hospital (840 Walnut Street) ophthalmology specialty. St. Christopher's Hospital for Children (1601 W. Allegheny Avenue) pediatric specialty (Tower Health-owned). PA-3 dialysis center roster F-flagged (F21-SD7-01).

**340B Drug Pricing Program scale.** 2024 340B spending: $66.3 billion record; DSH hospitals account for nearly $52 billion alone [340B Wikipedia citation]. ~33% of all US hospitals participate in 340B; 340B-purchased drugs account for 5% of all US medicines [340B Wikipedia]. Penn Medicine, Temple Health, Jefferson Health, CHOP all qualify as DSH 340B entities (disproportionate-share adjustment ≥11.75% per CRS R48696). 2024 contract pharmacy arrangements: 32,000+; average DSH covered entity uses 25 contract pharmacies [340B Wikipedia + Scoping Review citations]. **February 10, 2026 *AHA v. Kennedy* (D. Me., No. 25-cv-600)** vacated 340B Rebate Model Pilot. HRSA RFI closed April 20, 2026. HHS FY 2026 proposed budget would move 340B oversight from HRSA to CMS. PA-3 anchor hospital 340B pharmacy revenue magnitude F-flagged (F21-SD7-02).

**Medicare telehealth extension architecture.** Consolidated Appropriations Act 2026 (signed February 3, 2026) extended Medicare telehealth flexibilities through December 31, 2027 [Telehealth.org citation]. CY 2026 PFS Final Rule (October 31, 2025; CMS-1832-F) effective January 1, 2026 finalized permanent direct supervision via real-time two-way audio-visual telecommunications, permanent virtual teaching physician presence, permanent removal of telehealth frequency limits on subsequent inpatient/nursing facility visits [Quarles; Psychiatry.org citations]. Behavioral/mental health audio-only telehealth permanent under certain conditions [Telehealth.hhs.gov citation]. **September 30, 2025 telehealth waiver expiration; restored via November 12, 2025 continuing resolution through January 30, 2026; further extended through December 31, 2027 by CAA 2026** [Telehealth.org; ASCO; RCPA citations]. PA-3 telehealth utilization magnitude F-flagged (F21-SD7-03).

**Title X family planning scale and disposition.** FY 2025 + FY 2026 enacted appropriation: $286.5M + $286M per CRS IF10051 + KFF citations. National Title X: 2.8 million patients in 2023; nearly 4,000 clinics; 3,853 clinics as of 2023 per CRS IF10051. **2025-2026 disruption sequence:** March 31, 2025 withholding of 16 grantees / 22 grants / $65.8M / ~842,000 patients affected (~30% of Title X patient population per Guttmacher); Pennsylvania partially-affected; December 2025 restoration; March 13, 2026 HHS one-week guidance change removing "Quality Family Planning" standards and equity/inclusion goals; April 1, 2026 grants awarded including to Planned Parenthood affiliates. PA-3 Title X clinic roster and patient volume F-flagged (F21-SD7-04). Specific PA Title X funding allocation F-flagged (F21-SD7-05).

**IIJA reauthorization status.** IIJA P.L. 117-58 expires September 30, 2026 [CRS R48644 + R47573 + Funding Landscape citations]. As of May 2026, no reauthorization bill introduced [T4America citation]. CRS R47573 (April 14, 2026) projects 5-year FY2027 reauthorization gap $166B; 6-year gap $199B (HTF revenue-outlay imbalance). FY 2026 spending legislation rescinded ~$2.3B in IIJA allocations including ~$879M from NEVI EV charging [Funding Landscape; T4America]. BASICS Act introduced February 9, 2026 by Reps. Bresnahan (R-PA) + McDonald Rivet (D-MI) is legislative activity but not the master reauthorization vehicle [D13 MC-02 cross-reference]. House T&I Committee Chairman Sam Graves signaled "back to basics" approach; House T&I leaders Rouzer (R-NC) and Larsen (D-WA) targeted House bill passage by late summer / early fall 2026 [House T&I sources citation]. Cross-reference D13 G13-SD7-01 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR.

**OBBBA Section 71401 Rural Health Transformation Fund.** $50B / $10B annually 2026-2030; rural-only architecture; does not directly serve urban PA-3 healthcare-delivery infrastructure (cross-reference D21 SD5 G21-SD5-01). PA share and PA-3-relevant institutional flow-through F-flagged (cross-reference D21 SD5 F21-SD5-07).

**PA-3 sub-area variation.** Specialty service-line concentration at PA-3 academic medical centers operates in geographic distribution: HUP, Penn Presbyterian, Pennsylvania Hospital, CHOP, Jefferson primarily serve West Philadelphia Core and Center City interface (with South/Southwest interface for Jefferson Methodist); Temple Hospital and Episcopal serve North/Northwest Core; Einstein/Jefferson serves Northwest. Title X clinic geographic distribution F-flagged (F21-SD7-04). PA-3 ESRD dialysis center geographic distribution F-flagged (F21-SD7-01). The D13 cumulative-burden geography (Eastwick / Cobbs Creek South/Southwest concentration; North/Northwest Core + West Philadelphia Core equity-gap concentration) is the principal sub-area-variation analytical framework operative at SD7 (D13 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR cross-reference).

## Section 5: Aggregate Constituent Profiles

Profiles per M0 Standard 13 are aggregate, not named individuals.

**Profile 1: PA-3 oncology patient navigating Penn Abramson Cancer Center plus 340B pharmacy interface in West Philadelphia (MC58 candidate 340B Both/And).**

*Constituent type:* Working-age PA-3 resident; new oncology diagnosis (specific cancer type abstracted); commercial group health insurance (employer-sponsored); income approximately $58,000; West Philadelphia Core sub-area. *Specific PA-3 sub-area:* West Philadelphia Core. *Triggering event:* Diagnostic workup at primary care with referral to oncology specialty; treatment plan including chemotherapy and oncology-administered medications subject to 340B pricing architecture at DSH-qualifying anchor hospital.

*Pathway through the institutional system:* Constituent receives oncology consultation at Penn Abramson Cancer Center (cross-reference D2 SD7 substantive cancer architecture); treatment plan including chemotherapy infusion delivered at hospital outpatient setting; oncology-administered medications purchased by Penn Medicine at 340B-discounted price (typically 20-50% below standard wholesale acquisition cost per 340B Wikipedia citation); constituent's commercial insurance reimburses Penn Medicine at standard contracted rate; the spread between 340B price and standard reimbursement generates revenue used to cross-subsidize teaching, research, and uncompensated care at Penn Medicine [Daily Remedy 340B Coalition citation]. **February 10, 2026 *AHA v. Kennedy* (D. Me.)** vacated 340B Rebate Model Pilot Program Application Notice [HRSA citation]; HRSA RFI on potential rebate model closed April 20, 2026; HHS FY 2026 proposed budget would move 340B oversight from HRSA to CMS [Commonwealth Fund citation]. The MC58 candidate Both/And operationalizes: substantive 340B upfront-discount architecture continues operative at PA-3 anchor hospitals AND structural HRSA post-RFI rulemaking + potential HHS reorganization creates regulatory-architecture uncertainty for the oncology pharmacy revenue base.

*Illustrative figures with data provenance.* Penn Abramson Cancer Center at Penn Medicine HUP location [D]. 340B 2024 spending $66.3B with DSH hospitals at nearly $52B [340B Wikipedia citation; D]. 340B discount magnitude typically 20-50% below standard WAC [340B Wikipedia; Medicine to Market citations; D]. *AHA v. Kennedy* (D. Me., No. 25-cv-600) February 10, 2026 vacatur [HRSA citation; D]. HRSA RFI closed April 20, 2026 [HRSA citation; D].

*Outcome.* Constituent receives oncology treatment at Penn Abramson; commercial insurance covers treatment at network rates; out-of-pocket exposure governed by plan deductible-and-coinsurance architecture. The 340B pharmacy revenue cross-subsidy supports Penn Medicine's teaching-research-uncompensated-care architecture but operates under structural regulatory uncertainty pending HRSA post-RFI rulemaking.

**Profile 2: PA-3 ESRD patient navigating dialysis delivery under Medicare Part B in North Philadelphia.**

*Constituent type:* Working-age PA-3 resident; ESRD diagnosis; qualifying for Medicare Part B coverage regardless of age after qualifying period (42 U.S.C. § 1395rr); household income approximately $42,000; North/Northwest Core sub-area. *Triggering event:* End-stage renal disease requiring sustained dialysis; Medicare Part B 100% coverage architecture activates after qualifying period; selection of dialysis center within PA-3.

*Pathway through the institutional system:* Constituent receives ESRD diagnosis from primary care/specialty nephrology; coordinates transition to Medicare Part B coverage; selects dialysis center within PA-3 geographic accessibility (specific PA-3 dialysis center roster F-flagged, F21-SD7-01); receives in-center hemodialysis typically 3 times per week or home peritoneal dialysis depending on clinical and home-situation factors; Medicare Part B covers 100% of qualifying ESRD services. PA-3 dialysis centers operate under CMS ESRD Conditions for Coverage architecture (42 C.F.R. Part 494). Cross-reference D21 SD1 Medicare architecture for dual-eligible coordination where applicable.

*Illustrative figures with data provenance.* Medicare Part B ESRD coverage architecture [42 U.S.C. § 1395rr; D]. CMS ESRD Conditions for Coverage [42 C.F.R. Part 494; D]. In-center hemodialysis 3x/week typical schedule [SI structural inference from documented clinical patterns]. PA-3 dialysis center roster F-flagged (F21-SD7-01).

*Outcome.* Constituent receives sustained dialysis treatment under Medicare Part B coverage; geographic-access pattern depends on PA-3 dialysis center distribution. The ESRD coverage architecture is substantively stable; structural risk operates at OBBBA Medicaid delivery-side flow-through for the Medicaid-side Medicare-Medicaid dual-eligible architecture (cross-reference D21 SD1 G21-SD1-04 + D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02).

**Profile 3: PA-3 Title X-served patient navigating Pennsylvania partial-affected funding-disruption sequence in South Philadelphia (MC59 candidate Both/And).**

*Constituent type:* Working-age PA-3 resident; low-income (approximately 185% FPL); uninsured or under-insured; reproductive-health-services need (contraception, STI screening, cancer screening); South/Southwest sub-area. *Triggering event:* Sustained need for Title X-funded reproductive health services through 2025-2026 administrative-disruption sequence.

*Pathway through the institutional system:* Constituent accesses PA-3-serving Title X clinic (specific PA-3 Title X clinic roster F-flagged, F21-SD7-04). Pennsylvania named among partially-affected states in March 31, 2025 administration withholding of 16 grantees / 22 grants / $65.8M / ~842,000 patients nationally [Guttmacher citation]. NFPRHA lawsuit April 24, 2025; December 2025 HHS restoration of year-4 funds; March 13, 2026 HHS one-week guidance window removing "Quality Family Planning" standards and equity/inclusion programmatic goals [KFF; NPR citations]; April 1, 2026 Trump-administration FY 2026 Title X grants announced including to Planned Parenthood affiliates [Roll Call citation]. Constituent receives contraception, STI testing/treatment, cancer screening, wellness checks at Title X clinic; clinic operational stability affected by administrative-disruption sequence.

*Illustrative figures with data provenance.* Title X $286M FY 2026 enacted appropriation [CRS IF10051; KFF citations; D]. 16 grantees / 22 grants / $65.8M withholding March 31, 2025 [Guttmacher citation; D]. ~842,000 patients nationally affected (~30% of Title X patient population) [Guttmacher; KFF citations; D]. Pennsylvania partially-affected state [Guttmacher; 19th News citations; D]. March 13, 2026 HHS guidance removal of "Quality Family Planning" + equity/inclusion goals [KFF citation; D]. April 1, 2026 grants announced [Roll Call citation; D].

*Outcome.* Constituent receives Title X-funded reproductive health services at PA-3-serving clinic; clinic operational continuity is the load-bearing variable. The MC59 candidate Both/And operationalizes: substantive Title X $286M FY 2026 appropriation continues operative AND structural Trump-administration administrative-disruption sequence (March 2025 withholding + March 2026 one-week guidance window + potential 2019 Protect Life Rule reinstatement pending) creates operational instability at the clinic-services-delivery layer that affects sustained access. Cross-reference D21 SD5 G21-SD5-02 for FQHC + Title X interface.

**Profile 4: PA-3 dual-eligible navigating Medicare-extended-through-2027 telehealth architecture in Northwest Philadelphia (telehealth Both/And).**

*Constituent type:* PA-3 senior (age 70); dual-eligible Medicare + Medicaid CHC; mobility-limiting chronic condition; sustained telehealth utilization for primary care, specialty cardiology follow-up, and behavioral-health treatment; Northwest sub-area. *Triggering event:* Sustained telehealth utilization across primary care + specialty + behavioral health; Medicare telehealth flexibility extension architecture.

*Pathway through the institutional system:* Constituent accesses Medicare telehealth services under Section 1834(m) extended through December 31, 2027 [CAA 2026 citation]: home-based primary care telehealth without geographic restriction; audio-only cardiology follow-up where appropriate; behavioral-health telehealth without in-person visit requirement (grandfather provision applicable if services began on or before January 30, 2026) [CMS Telehealth FAQ 2/26/26 citation]; FQHC distant-site provider access via G2025 billing code through December 31, 2027 [Telehealth.hhs.gov citation]. CY 2026 PFS Final Rule permanent provisions: direct supervision via audio-visual telecommunications; virtual teaching physician presence; permanent removal of telehealth frequency limits on subsequent inpatient/nursing facility visits [Quarles; Psychiatry.org citations]. PA Act 98 of 2022 permits audio-only telehealth for PA-state Medicaid coverage in outpatient psychiatric and SUD clinics (cross-reference D21 SD6).

*Illustrative figures with data provenance.* Section 1834(m) Medicare telehealth statutory architecture [D]. CAA 2026 extension through December 31, 2027 [Telehealth.org; Telehealth.hhs.gov citations; D]. CY 2026 PFS Final Rule permanent provisions [Quarles; Psychiatry.org citations; D]. Audio-only behavioral/mental telehealth permanent under conditions [Telehealth.hhs.gov citation; D]. PA Act 98 of 2022 [Source 86 D21 SD6 cross-reference; D].

*Outcome.* Constituent receives sustained telehealth-mediated care across primary, specialty, and behavioral health domains through December 31, 2027. The telehealth Both/And operationalizes: substantive telehealth-access architecture continues operative through 2027 (with select permanent provisions for behavioral health audio-only and direct supervision) AND structural reversion-to-pre-PHE-architecture risk operates after December 31, 2027 absent further Congressional action — the December 31, 2025 EPTC expiration precedent (cross-reference D21 SD3 G21-SD3-01) demonstrates legislative-deadline-driven disruption pattern that informs forward planning.

## Section 6: Conversational Narrative Note

The most analytically important feature visible at SD7 is the convergence. The PA-3 healthcare-delivery architecture in 2026-2027 operates at the intersection of six concurrent federal-policy-cycle mechanisms that each have their own intended effect and none of which intends the cumulative outcome on healthcare-delivery-infrastructure-fragility at the PA-3 level. The IIJA reauthorization September 30, 2026 inflection point operates as a discrete legislative deadline affecting transportation-and-infrastructure architecture with healthcare-delivery-adjacent flow-through; D13 SD7 G13-SD7-01 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR documents that as of May 2026 no reauthorization bill has been introduced and that the Highway Trust Fund balance is projected at ~$45 billion (seven months of average outlays) at end of FY 2026 [CRS R47573 citation]. The Medicare telehealth flexibility extension through December 31, 2027 under Consolidated Appropriations Act 2026 (signed February 3, 2026) sustains delivery architecture for ~22 months but creates another discrete legislative deadline at 12/31/27 [Telehealth.org; Telehealth.hhs.gov citations]. The 340B Rebate Model Pilot post-court-vacatur (*AHA v. Kennedy*, D. Me., February 10, 2026) plus HRSA RFI closed April 20, 2026 plus HHS FY 2026 proposed budget move of 340B oversight from HRSA to CMS operates as an administrative-architecture-uncertainty mechanism affecting PA-3 anchor hospital pharmacy revenue base [HRSA; Commonwealth Fund citations]. The Title X 2025-2026 administrative-disruption sequence (March 2025 withholding → December 2025 restoration → March 13, 2026 one-week guidance window → April 1, 2026 FY 2026 grants announced) operates as a sustained administrative-pressure mechanism on PA-3-serving Title X clinics [Guttmacher; KFF; NPR; Roll Call citations]. The OBBBA Medicaid delivery-side flow-through (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02; D21 SD5 G21-SD5-01) operates across multiple architectural layers affecting BH-MCO capitation, provider-tax safe-harbor stepdown, 6-month redetermination procedural-loss, and noncitizen Medicaid restrictions. The MHPAEA 2024 Final Rule Trump-administration partial non-enforcement disposition (cross-reference D21 SD6 G21-SD6-02) operates at the federal-floor parity layer. Each mechanism is independently legitimate at the federal-policy-cycle level; none intends the cumulative healthcare-delivery-architecture-fragility outcome that emerges from their concurrent operation.

The most common misunderstanding about specialty and cross-cutting healthcare delivery in 2026 is that the post-PHE telehealth-flexibility architecture is now stable. It is not. The post-COVID-PHE telehealth architecture has operated under repeated short-term extensions — September 30, 2025 expiration; restored via November 12, 2025 continuing resolution through January 30, 2026; further extended via CAA 2026 (signed February 3, 2026) through December 31, 2027 [ASCO; RCPA; Telehealth.org citations]. Three permanent provisions stand on solid statutory ground: behavioral/mental health audio-only telehealth (permanent under certain conditions); direct supervision via real-time audio-visual telecommunications (CY 2026 PFS Final Rule); virtual teaching physician presence (CY 2026 PFS Final Rule) [CMS Telehealth FAQ 2/26/26; Quarles; Psychiatry.org citations]. The remainder operates under the December 31, 2027 sunset. Stakeholders are increasingly pushing for stand-alone permanent telehealth legislation to reduce the disruption caused by reliance on short-term funding bills [Telehealth.org citation]. The Telehealth Both/And operationalizes: substantive telehealth access continues operative through 2027 AND structural reversion-to-pre-PHE-architecture risk operates after 12/31/27.

The human consequence visible in 2026 concentrates at three convergence layers. First, PA-3 anchor hospital specialty service delivery (oncology at Penn Abramson; cardiology at Penn Heart and Vascular; pediatric specialty at CHOP; neurology at Jefferson Vickie and Jack Farber Institute) operates with a 340B pharmacy revenue cross-subsidy supporting teaching, research, and uncompensated care [Daily Remedy citation] under the structural regulatory uncertainty of the post-court-vacatur HRSA RFI rulemaking trajectory plus potential HHS reorganization moving 340B oversight to CMS. Second, PA-3 Title X-served patients experience administrative-disruption-sequence operational instability while the $286M FY 2026 appropriation continues operative at the statutory level; the March 13, 2026 HHS one-week guidance window removing "Quality Family Planning" standards and equity/inclusion programmatic goals represents a substantive shift in program design even as funding flows [KFF; NPR; Health Law Policy Brief citations]. Third, PA-3 healthcare-delivery-adjacent infrastructure (SEPTA transit to academic medical centers; FHWA road infrastructure affecting EMS routing) faces the September 30, 2026 IIJA expiration inflection point absent reauthorization or extension; the cross-domain principal-anchor cumulative-burden geography concentration in Eastwick/Cobbs Creek (D13 G13-SD4-05; G13-SD6-05) plus North/Northwest Core + West Philadelphia Core sub-areas compounds healthcare-access disparities with the upstream environmental-burden architecture (cross-reference D13 + D6 PRINCIPAL ANCHORS).

The most analytically important federal-engagement feature visible at SD7 is the legislative-deadline architecture. Five of the six concurrent federal-policy-cycle mechanisms above operate at discrete deadlines or rulemaking horizons through 2026-2027: IIJA expiration September 30, 2026; Medicare telehealth extension end December 31, 2027; 340B Rebate Pilot post-RFI rulemaking 2026-2027; OBBBA Section 71109 effective October 1, 2026; OBBBA Section 71107 6-month redetermination beginning December 2026 (D21 SD2). Federal House representation operates at each deadline directly through legislation, appropriation, and oversight authority; the legislative-deadline architecture means single-mechanism engagement may address one mechanism without addressing the cumulative architecture per the MC55 SD5 emergent-from-interaction HOM 2nd-instance shape diagnostic. PA-state-level texture engagement at PA Medicaid PPS rate-setting (PA DHS), PA OMHSAS Behavioral HealthChoices contracting, PA Insurance Department MHPAEA enforcement, PA Hospital and Healthsystem Association advocacy, and PA Department of Health hospital licensure operates at the complementary state-level locus. The cross-domain principal-anchor deferral architecture (D13 + D6) means that the deepest substantive analytical content on IIJA inflection, cumulative-burden geography, PA-state-fiscal asymmetry, and AHERA criminal-enforcement at SDP school-environmental-health intersection is owned by upstream domains and inherited at SD7 as ESTABLISHED CONTEXT.

**G21-SD7-01: IIJA reauthorization September 30, 2026 inflection point delivery-side flow-through to PA-3 healthcare-delivery-adjacent infrastructure (MC57 candidate Both/And; D13 G13-SD7-01 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR cross-reference).**
*Finding — substantive infrastructure continuity:* IIJA (P.L. 117-58) signed November 15, 2021 authorized $1.2 trillion total / $550B new investment FY2022-FY2026 supporting SEPTA transit to PA-3 academic medical centers, FHWA road infrastructure affecting EMS routing and patient access, DCNR LWCF parks-and-recreation infrastructure affecting community health architectures (cross-reference D13 verified file). FTA authorized up to $108B for federal public transportation programs ($91B guaranteed funding) [FTA citation]. Formula programs (highway, bridge, transit) continue at IIJA levels through FY2026 [Funding Landscape citation].
*Finding — structural reauthorization inflection-point risk:* IIJA expires **September 30, 2026** [CRS R48644; CRS R47573 citations]. As of May 2026, no reauthorization bill introduced [T4America; Funding Landscape citations]. CRS R47573 (April 14, 2026) projects 5-year FY2027 reauthorization gap $166B; 6-year gap $199B (HTF revenue-outlay imbalance); HTF balance projected at ~$45B end of FY 2026 (~7 months avg outlays); CBO projects FTA may delay payments Q3 FY2027, FHWA Q2 FY2028 absent reauthorization or general fund transfers. House T&I Committee Chairman Sam Graves (R-MO) "back to basics" approach; House T&I leaders Rouzer (R-NC) and Larsen (D-WA) targeted House bill passage by late summer / early fall 2026 [House T&I source citation]; BASICS Act introduced February 9, 2026 by Reps. Bresnahan (R-PA) and McDonald Rivet (D-MI) is legislative activity but not master vehicle [D13 MC-02]. Congressional extensions are the expected mechanism absent reauthorization [D13 verified file]. FY 2026 spending legislation rescinded ~$2.3B in IIJA allocations including ~$879M from NEVI EV charging [Funding Landscape citation].
*Evidence type:* [D] for IIJA statutory architecture and September 30, 2026 expiration; [D] for no-reauthorization-bill status; [D] for House T&I leadership statements; [D] for HTF balance projections per CRS.
*Confidence:* HIGH for structural feature; MEDIUM for forward reauthorization trajectory.
*Representation implication:* Federal House representation has direct legislative authority on IIJA reauthorization architecture; House T&I Committee oversight responsibility direct. PA House delegation engagement (Bresnahan R-PA among bipartisan BASICS Act introducers) operative at legislative-text-development layer. Cross-reference D13 SD7 G13-SD7-01 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR for substantive transportation-program analysis. *T21-SD7-01: IIJA reauthorization legislative trajectory through September 30, 2026 expiration; congressional extension mechanism if no reauthorization enacted; multi-year vs short-term reauthorization architecture.*

**G21-SD7-02: 340B Drug Pricing Program Rebate Model Pilot court vacatur + HRSA RFI post-April-20-2026 rulemaking trajectory at PA-3 anchor hospital pharmacy revenue architecture (MC58 candidate Both/And; cross-reference D21 SD5 G21-SD5-01 + G21-SD5-02).**
*Finding — substantive 340B continuity at PA-3 anchor hospitals:* Penn Medicine, Temple Health, Jefferson Health, CHOP all qualify as DSH 340B entities (disproportionate-share adjustment ≥11.75% per CRS R48696). 2024 340B spending: $66.3 billion (record); DSH hospitals account for nearly $52 billion [340B Wikipedia citation]. 340B discount magnitude typically 20-50% below standard wholesale acquisition cost [340B Wikipedia; Medicine to Market citations]. 340B revenue used to cross-subsidize teaching, research, and uncompensated care at academic medical centers [Daily Remedy 340B Coalition citation]. Contract pharmacy architecture: 32,000+ arrangements (2024); average DSH covered entity uses 25 contract pharmacies [340B Wikipedia + Scoping Review citations].
*Finding — structural post-court-vacatur uncertainty:* **February 10, 2026 *AHA v. Kennedy* (D. Me., No. 25-cv-600)** vacated and remanded to HHS the 340B Rebate Model Pilot Program Application Notice (90 Fed. Reg. 36,163; August 1, 2025), Corrected Application Notice (90 Fed. Reg. 38,165; August 7, 2025), and manufacturer-application approvals (announced October 30 - November 14, 2025) [HRSA 340B page citation]. HRSA RFI on potential rebate model use closed April 20, 2026 [HRSA citation]. HHS FY 2026 proposed budget would move 340B oversight from HRSA to CMS [Commonwealth Fund citation]. Manufacturers including AstraZeneca, Eli Lilly, Novartis, Bristol Myers Squibb have attempted to restrict 340B discounts to contract pharmacies; HRSA and courts have largely sided against manufacturers; legal landscape continues to evolve [Medicine to Market citation]. Several states have enacted contract-pharmacy-protection laws; Eighth Circuit upheld Arkansas's law March 2024; Supreme Court likely to address within two years if circuits split [Daily Remedy citation].
*Evidence type:* [D] for *AHA v. Kennedy* court vacatur; [D] for HRSA RFI; [D] for HHS FY 2026 proposed budget reorganization; [D] for 2024 spending magnitudes.
*Confidence:* HIGH for structural feature; MEDIUM for forward HRSA post-RFI rulemaking trajectory.
*Representation implication:* Federal House representation has direct legislative authority on 340B statutory amendments (cross-reference D21 SD5 H.R. 7391 bipartisan); oversight authority on HRSA + CMS rulemaking trajectory and on potential HHS reorganization. *T21-SD7-02: HRSA post-April-2026-RFI rulemaking trajectory through 2026-2027; potential CMS-administered 340B architecture; H.R. 7391 Senate progression.*

**G21-SD7-04: Medicare telehealth post-December 31, 2027 reversion risk + select permanent provisions (Telehealth Both/And).**
*Finding — substantive telehealth extension through 12/31/27:* Medicare telehealth flexibilities extended through December 31, 2027 under Consolidated Appropriations Act 2026 signed February 3, 2026 [Telehealth.org; Telehealth.hhs.gov citations]: home-based telehealth without geographic restriction; audio-only non-behavioral telehealth; FQHCs and RHCs as distant-site providers via G2025; expanded distant-site practitioner list; behavioral/mental telehealth without in-person visit requirement (grandfather provision for beneficiaries who began services on or before January 30, 2026) [CMS Telehealth FAQ 2/26/26 citation]. CY 2026 PFS Final Rule (CMS-1832-F, October 31, 2025) **permanent** provisions: (a) direct supervision via real-time two-way audio-visual telecommunications; (b) virtual teaching physician presence; (c) removal of telehealth frequency limits on subsequent inpatient/nursing facility visits; (d) behavioral/mental health audio-only telehealth permanent under certain conditions [Quarles; Psychiatry.org; Telehealth.hhs.gov citations].
*Finding — structural December 31, 2027 reversion risk:* Absent further Congressional action, Medicare telehealth flexibilities revert to pre-PHE architecture on December 31, 2027: geographic and originating-site restrictions; limited eligible practitioner list; non-behavioral audio-only architecture sunsets. Repeated short-term extension pattern (September 30, 2025 expiration → November 12, 2025 CR through January 30, 2026 → CAA 2026 through December 31, 2027) creates planning-horizon disruption [ASCO; RCPA; Telehealth.org citations]. Stakeholders are increasingly pushing for stand-alone permanent telehealth legislation [Telehealth.org citation].
*Evidence type:* [D] for current extension architecture and permanent provisions; [SI] for structural reversion-risk pattern.
*Confidence:* HIGH for current architecture; MEDIUM for forward 2027-end trajectory.

## Section 8: Representation Question Closure

**What the legal framework provides or promises.** SD7 surfaces a substantial federal framework architecture for cross-cutting healthcare delivery: IIJA P.L. 117-58 authorizing $1.2 trillion infrastructure investment through September 30, 2026; Medicare Part B specialty service-line coverage architecture; Medicare Part B ESRD coverage 42 U.S.C. § 1395rr; Medicare telehealth extension through December 31, 2027 under CAA 2026; CY 2026 PFS Final Rule permanent direct supervision and virtual teaching physician provisions; 340B Drug Pricing Program 42 U.S.C. § 256b with $66.3B 2024 spending including ~$52B DSH at academic medical centers; Title X PHSA 42 U.S.C. §§ 300 to 300a-6 with $286M FY 2026 appropriated; Veterans Choice / Community Care Network 38 U.S.C. § 1703; OSHA BBP + HazCom + workplace violence guidance; AHERA + TSCA Title II asbestos compliance (D6 MC-03 G6-SD4-02 cross-reference); ACGME accreditation and Medicare GME funding architecture. Pennsylvania's framework: PA Health Care Facilities Act; PA Public Welfare Code; PA MCARE Act; PA healthcare workforce licensure across multiple titles. Cross-domain principal anchors inherited from D13 (IIJA + cumulative-burden + PA-fiscal-asymmetry) and D6 (MC-03 AHERA + MC-04/32 EJ-divergence).

**What PA-3 constituents actually receive.** Specialty service-line delivery at Penn Medicine (Abramson Cancer Center; Penn Heart and Vascular; HUP; Pennsylvania Hospital; Penn Presbyterian), Temple Health (Temple Lung Center; cardiology; Episcopal), Jefferson Health (Sidney Kimmel Cancer Center; Vickie and Jack Farber Institute; Methodist trauma), CHOP (pediatric specialty plus 50+ Care Network locations), Wills Eye, St. Christopher's Hospital for Children. Medicare telehealth access through December 31, 2027 with select permanent provisions (behavioral/mental audio-only; direct supervision via audio-visual; virtual teaching physician). 340B pharmacy revenue cross-subsidy supporting PA-3 academic medical center teaching, research, and uncompensated care at substantive magnitude. Title X-funded reproductive health services at PA-3-serving Title X clinics with administrative-disruption-sequence operational instability through 2025-2026. ESRD dialysis under Medicare Part B 100% coverage at PA-3 dialysis centers. Veterans health care via VA Philadelphia Medical Center and Veterans Choice / Community Care Network. PA-3 healthcare-delivery-adjacent infrastructure (SEPTA transit; FHWA roads; community-health-supportive infrastructure) operates through IIJA-funded programs facing September 30, 2026 expiration.

**What accounts for the gap.** Six structural features account for the gap between framework and received delivery: (a) the IIJA reauthorization September 30, 2026 inflection-point creates planning-horizon disruption for healthcare-delivery-adjacent infrastructure (G21-SD7-01; D13 G13-SD7-01 PRINCIPAL ANCHOR); (b) the 340B Rebate Model Pilot post-court-vacatur HRSA RFI rulemaking trajectory plus potential HHS reorganization moving 340B oversight from HRSA to CMS creates regulatory-architecture uncertainty affecting PA-3 anchor hospital pharmacy revenue (G21-SD7-02; MC58 candidate Both/And); (c) the Title X 2025-2026 administrative-disruption sequence (March 2025 withholding → December 2025 restoration → March 13, 2026 one-week guidance window → April 1, 2026 FY 2026 grants → potential 2019 Protect Life Rule reinstatement) creates operational instability at PA-3-serving Title X clinics while $286M appropriation continues at the statutory level (G21-SD7-03; MC59 candidate Both/And); (d) the Medicare telehealth extension through December 31, 2027 stabilizes near-term delivery architecture but creates structural reversion-to-pre-PHE-architecture risk after 12/31/27 absent further Congressional action; select permanent provisions stand on solid statutory ground (G21-SD7-04); (e) the PA-3 specialty network adequacy gaps and primary-care workforce-pipeline gap downstream of specialty-selection economics under ACGME GME architecture (G21-SD7-05; D21 SD4 G21-SD4-02 cross-reference); (f) the cross-domain principal-anchor architecture (D13 cumulative-burden geography concentration in Eastwick/Cobbs Creek South/Southwest + North/Northwest Core + West Philadelphia Core equity-gap; D13 PA-state-fiscal-architectural asymmetry; D6 MC-03 SDP AHERA DOJ DPA at school-environmental-health intersection; D6 MC32 EJ-divergence affecting SAMHSA + Title X targeted-population funding) operates at the upstream-domain layer affecting PA-3 healthcare-delivery interface (G21-SD7-06). The cumulative effect surfaces the SD7 convergence-layer architecture: six concurrent federal-policy-cycle mechanisms operating with their own intended effects against PA-3 healthcare-delivery infrastructure through 2026-2027, with cumulative outcome no single mechanism intends — the MC55 SD5 emergent-from-interaction HOM 2nd-instance shape diagnostic provides the disciplinary structure for analyzing this convergence at the project-wide level.

**What the gap means for the representation analysis.** Federal House representation operates at: (a) IIJA reauthorization legislation and House T&I Committee oversight; (b) 340B statutory amendments and HRSA + CMS rulemaking oversight (H.R. 7391 cross-reference); (c) Title X appropriation and HHS OPA implementation oversight; (d) Medicare telehealth statutory architecture and CMS PFS rulemaking oversight; (e) Medicare GME architecture and specialty-distribution-incentive design; (f) OBBBA technical corrections affecting Medicaid delivery-side flow-through (cross-reference D21 SD2 G21-SD2-02); (g) MHPAEA enforcement architecture oversight (cross-reference D21 SD6 G21-SD6-02); (h) Veterans Choice / Community Care Network oversight (cross-reference D24); (i) cross-domain principal-anchor engagement at D13 IIJA + cumulative-burden geography + PA-fiscal-asymmetry trajectories and at D6 MC-03 AHERA federal enforcement architecture. PA-state-level texture engagement at PA Medicaid PPS rate-setting, PA OMHSAS Behavioral HealthChoices contracting, PA Insurance Department PA Mental Health Parity Act enforcement, PA Hospital and Healthsystem Association advocacy, PA Department of Health hospital licensure, and PA Department of Drug and Alcohol Programs is the complementary locus. SD7 closes Phase 1 architecturally at the convergence layer where the per-SD load-bearing findings cohere; the MC55 SD5 emergent-from-interaction HOM 2nd-instance shape diagnostic + the MC53 / MC54 / MC56 / MC60 / MC61 Both/And designations + the MC57 / MC58 / MC59 candidate designations at SD7 cohere with the commitment-vs-outcome HOM 7th-instance candidate at SD4 G21-SD4-01 into the project-wide D21 analytical architecture for Phase 2 OUTPUT synthesis.
